Glauber quark and gluon contributions to quark energy loss at next-to-leading order and next-to-leading twist
Amit Kumar, Gojko Vujanovic

Abstract
The higher-twist (HT) formalism is used at O ⁡ ( α s 2 ) O(\alpha^{2}_{s}) to compute all possible medium-induced single-scattering emission kernels for an incoming highly energetic and virtual quark traversing the nuclear environment. The effects of the heavy-quark mass scale are taken into account [ Phys. Rev. C 94, 054902 (2016) ] both in the initial state as well as in the final state, along with interactions involving both in-medium Glauber gluons and quarks [ Nucl. Phys. A 793, 128 (2007) ], as well as coherence effects [ Phys. Rev. C 105, 024908 (2022) ]. As this study is a continuation of our work on medium-induced photon production [ Phys. Rev. C 112, 025204 (2025) ], the general factorization procedure for e e - A A deep-inelastic scattering is still used. An incoming quark energy loss in the nuclear medium yields four possible scattering kernels 𝒦 i \mathcal{K}_{i} with the following final states: (i) q + g q+g , (ii) g + g g+g , (iii) q + q ¯ ′ q+\bar{q}^{\prime} , where the quark q q may have a flavor different from the antiquark q ¯ ′ \bar{q}^{\prime} , and (iv) q + q ′ q+q^{\prime} , where, again, q q may have a flavor different from q ′ q^{\prime} . The collisional kernels include full phase factors from all non-vanishing diagrams and complete first-order derivative in the longitudinal direction ( k − k^{-} ) as well as second-order derivative in the transverse momentum ( k ⟂ k_{\perp} ) gradient expansion. Furthermore, in-medium parton distribution functions and the related jet transport coefficients have a hard transverse-momentum dependence (of the emitted quark or gluon) present within the phase factor.



I
Introduction

Ultrarelativistic heavy-ions collisions carried out at the Relativistic Heavy-Ion Collider (RHIC) and the Large Hadron Collider (LHC) produce a deconfined state of quarks and gluons, called quark-gluon plasma (QGP). One of the primary goals of these collisions is to constrain properties of QGP, through, e.g., the modifications it imparts on high-energy quark- and gluon-initiated jets. Jet evolution in the QGP is a multiscale process, and different physics are involved at different virtuality scales. While highly-energetic, nearly on-shell jet partons are described through effective Boltzmann transport, which includes the hard thermal loop (HTL) formalism supplemented by Landau-Pomeranchuk-Migdal (LPM) resummation
Arnold et al. 2000
;
Arnold et al. 2003a
;
Arnold et al. 2003b
, the goal of this contribution is to explore the evolution of jet partons using the higher-twist (HT) formalism in the single-scattering limit
Guo and Wang 2000
;
Wang and Guo 2001
;
Majumder 2012
for high energy and virtual partons.
1
1

1

While modifications to the radiation spectrum induced by single scattering can happen at any virtuality, as virtuality is lowered multiple scatterings become important
Majumder and Muller 2008
;
Majumder 2012
. Thus, for sufficiently short-lived partons, single scattering is sufficient do describe their in-medium radiation, as done here.
In addition to these theoretical developments, there has also been a surge in various Monte Carlo implementations of these approaches, such as MATTER
Majumder 2013a
;
Cao and Majumder 2020
at high virtuality, with LBT
Cao et al. 2016
;
Cao et al. 2017
and MARTINI
Schenke et al. 2009
at lower virtuality, while the JETSCAPE framework
Putschke et al. 2019
;
Kumar et al. 2020
has enabled a more holistic simulation to be devised, better covering the virtuality-dependent dynamics of jet energy loss in a nuclear environment. The JETSCAPE framework also incorporates event-by-event simulations of the nuclear medium, including hydrodynamical simulation of the QGP, along with pre-hydrodynamical simulations and hadronic transport, allowing sophisticated Bayesian model-to-data comparisons to be conducted
Ehlers et al. 2025
. To push Bayesian analysis further, jet-medium transport coefficients should be explored in a more discerning way by separating the gluonic from the fermionic contributions to jet-medium transport coefficients (or accounting for both of them), as is done herein.

Following a collision of two nuclei at ultra-relativistic energies, the composition of the nuclear medium at the onset of its expansion is governed by the nuclear-modified parton distribution functions (nPDFs) of the colliding nuclei. The initial energy-momentum tensor is dominated by gluons
Navas et al. 2024
, thus forming a glasma. The particle composition changes at later stages, as quark-antiquark pairs are dynamically generated, leading to hydrodynamization. In the hydrodynamic regime, the lattice QCD equation is used
Heffernan et al. 2024
, and quarks and gluons are assumed to have reached their thermodynamic occupation numbers. Given that jets are sensitive to the entire dynamical evolution of the nuclear medium, they probe flavor hydrodynamization dynamics. A pre-hydroynamical model that accounts for flavor hydrodynamization can be implemented inside of a realistic simulation of heavy-ion collisions using the modular JETSCAPE framework, enabling the next generation of jet-medium Bayesian analysis.

The production investigated herein is induced by processes illustrated in Fig.
1
, where the HT formalism in the single-scattering-induced radiation limit is used to calculate their production kernels
\mathcal{K}
. The first kernel
\mathcal{K}_{1}
captures processes involving a Glauber gluon
Idilbi and Majumder 2009
exchange with the nuclear medium, as depicted in the blob diagram Fig.
1
(a). The second kernel
\mathcal{K}_{2}
involves an annihilation process between the jet quark and the in-medium antiquark (or vice versa) thus giving rise to quark-to-gluon conversion depicted in Fig.
1
(b). A subset of diagrams in Fig.
1
were already explored in our previous calculation
Kumar and Vujanovic 2025
. However, interactions in the medium can also involve virtual gluons giving either a quark-quark final state, encoded in the third kernel
\mathcal{K}_{3}
, or a quark-antiquark final state encapsulated within the fourth kernel
\mathcal{K}_{4}
, as shown in Fig.
1
(c-d), respectively. The diagrams in Fig.
1
will be referred to as Kumar-Vujanovic (KV) kernels. As was the case in our previous study
Kumar and Vujanovic 2025
, there will be Glauber gluon
Idilbi and Majumder 2009
and quark contributions to in-medium (i) jet transverse momentum broadening, as well as (ii) longitudinal drag, taking into account the heavy-quark mass scale
Abir and Majumder 2016
, coherence effects
Sirimanna et al. 2022
, together with fermion-to-boson conversion processes
Schafer et al. 2007
.

(a)

A gluon and quark in the final state.

(b)

Two gluons in the final state.

(c)

Quark anti-quark in the final state.

(d)

Two quarks in the final state.

Figure 1
:
Scattering diagrams with one emission and one scattering for a quark initiating jet.

This paper is organized into several sections. Section
II
describes the separation of the leptonic tensors and the hadronic tensor in Deep-Inelastic Scattering (DIS), including the various scattering kernels explored here. Section
III
provides details on the calculation of one diagram that contributes to the
q\to q+g
scattering kernel, with the aim of precisely introducing the power-counting and approximation scheme used in this work. All other diagrams contributing to the various collisional kernels are presented in the appendices. Section
IV
gives the results for the four scattering kernels in Fig.
1
, their collinear expansion along with a discussion, is presented in Sec.
V
, while a summary and an outlook are given in Sec.
VI
.

II
Hadronic Tensor in Deep Inelastic Scattering

Deep Inelastic Scattering (DIS) reactions can be used to study (i) how jets exchange their energy and momentum with a nuclear environment, and (ii) how the jet’s particle-composition changes in the nuclear medium. The fundamental DIS-type reaction explored herein is:

e^{-}(\ell_{\rm in})+A(\mathcal{P})\to e^{-}(\ell_{\rm out})+X.

(1)

where an incoming electron with momentum
\ell_{\rm in}
collides with a nucleus
A
with momentum
\mathcal{P}
, giving an outgoing electron with momentum
\ell_{\rm out}
and a final state
X
. This reaction is illustrated in Fig.
2
, where
X
refers to the state on the far right-hand side of that figure. As depicted, Fig.
2
is a leading order (LO) process, where all possible states
X
are implicitly accounted for.

Figure 2
:
A schematic diagram of deep-inelastic scattering between an electron and a nucleon inside the nucleus. The virtual photon carries momentum
q
, whereas the struck quark carries momentum
p
. The nucleus momentum is
\mathcal{P}=AP
, where
P
is the momentum of the nucleon.

At next-to-leading order (NLO), the substructure of
X
is explored more explicitly by allowing the highly off-shell quark, which has momentum
p_{1}
in Fig.
2
, to produce identified particles in the (perturbative) final state depicted in Fig.
1
. Figure
1
(a) gives a medium-modified gluon radiation spectrum, which is changed due to coherent single-scattering interactions with a Glauber gluon
Idilbi and Majumder 2009
; and is the only vacuum process, of the form
q\to q+g
, that is modified in the medium. Interactions with Glauber quarks in nuclear matter shown in Fig.
1
(b–d) thus open novel channels that are not available in vacuum. The processes in Fig.
1
(b) correspond to
q\to g+g
(through in-medium annihilation with a Glauber quark, of course), while Fig.
1
(c) corresponds either to
q\to q+\bar{q}^{\prime}
or
q\to q^{\prime}+\bar{q}^{\prime}
, where
q^{\prime}
may be a different quark flavor than
q
. Finally, Fig.
1
(d) corresponds to
q\to q+q^{\prime}
, where
q^{\prime}
may be a different quark flavor from
q
once more.

The general expression for the cross section of the reaction in Eq.
1
is

\displaystyle E_{\ell_{\rm out}}\frac{d^{3}\sigma}{d^{3}\ell_{\rm out}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha^{2}_{\rm em}}{2\pi s}\frac{L_{\mu\nu}W^{\mu\nu}}{Q^{4}},

\displaystyle L^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}{\rm Tr}\left[\not{\ell}_{\rm in}\gamma^{\mu}\not{\ell}_{\rm out}\gamma^{\nu}\right],

(2)

where
\alpha_{\rm em}=e^{2}/(4\pi)
is the fine-structure constant,
s=\left(\mathcal{P}+\ell_{\rm in}\right)^{2}
is the usual Mandelstam variable,
\not{\ell}=\ell_{\mu}\gamma^{\mu}
with
\gamma^{\mu}
are the usual Dirac matrices, while
q^{\mu}=\ell^{\mu}_{\rm out}-\ell^{\mu}_{\rm in}
and gives
q^{\mu}q_{\mu}=-Q^{2}
. The hadronic tensor
W^{\mu\nu}
in Eq.
2
contains the QCD portion of the interaction and is the main interest here. It is defined as a complete matrix element given by
Collins 2011

\displaystyle W^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{X}\delta^{(4)}(p_{X}-\mathcal{P}-q)\langle AP,S|j^{\mu}(0)|X\rangle\langle X|j^{\nu}(0)|AP,S\rangle,

\displaystyle j^{\mu}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{f}e_{f}\bar{\psi}_{f}(x)\gamma^{\mu}\psi_{f}(x),

(3)

where the sum over
X
denotes the usual Lorentz-invariant sum (and integral) over all hadronic states, and
S
is the spin state of the target. The fractional charge
e_{f}=2/3
for the up, charm, and top quarks, while being
e_{f}=-1/3
for the down, strange, and bottom quarks. Note that the electric unit charge
e
has been accounted for in the
\alpha^{2}_{\rm em}
term on the right-hand side of Eq.
2
, therefore neither
L_{\mu\nu}
nor
W^{\mu\nu}
contain any
\alpha_{\rm em}
. The light-cone coordinates are chosen (cf.
Kumar and Vujanovic 2025
for details) where

\displaystyle q^{\mu}=\left[q^{+},q^{-},{\bf q}_{\perp}={\bf 0}_{\perp}\right]=\left[\frac{-Q^{2}}{2q^{-}},q^{-},0,0\right],

(4)

such that the
q^{+}
and
q^{-}
components of the incoming virtual momentum are large, i.e.,
O(1)
, giving
q^{\mu}\sim[O(1),O(1),{\bf 0}_{\perp}]Q
. Note that the hard scale is given by
Q=\sqrt{-q^{2}}\gg\Lambda_{QCD}
.

In this paper, we focus on the situation where the highly virtual photon carrying four momentum
q^{\mu}
strikes a nucleon traveling in the positive
z
-direction. In this setup, the struck quark has a very small
p^{-}\sim\lambda^{2}Q
momentum component — where the dimensionless parameter
\lambda
is a small quantity
\lambda^{2}\llless 1
— while the large momentum component is
p^{+}\sim Q
, resulting in the four momentum scaling:
p^{\mu}\sim\left[O(1),O\left(\lambda^{2}\right),{\bf 0}_{\perp}\right]Q
. The momentum components of the quark after the scattering are organized as
p^{\mu}_{1}\sim\left[O\left(\lambda^{2}\right),O(1),{\bf 0}_{\perp}\right]Q
.
2
2

2

Note that the mass
M
of the struck quark can be sizable
\frac{M}{Q}\sim O(\lambda)
, hence
p^{\mu}_{1}=\left[\frac{M^{2}-Q^{2}+2\left(p^{+}q^{-}-\frac{M^{2}}{2p^{+}}\frac{Q^{2}}{2q^{-}}\right)}{2p^{-}_{1}},p^{-}_{1},{\bf 0}_{\perp}\right]
.
Thus,
\lambda
is used as a small scale to establish a perturbative series expansion.

The real final-state parton radiation spectrum contained within
W^{\mu\nu}
is divided as follows:

\displaystyle\frac{dW^{\mu\nu}}{dy}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{dW^{\mu\nu}_{0}}{dy}+\sum^{4}_{i=1}\frac{dW^{\mu\nu}_{i}}{dy},

where
\frac{dW^{\mu\nu}_{0}}{dy}
is the vacuum contribution to gluon radiation from the jet, while the in-medium correction is enclosed is
\sum_{i}\frac{dW^{\mu\nu}_{i}}{dy}
and is depicted in Fig.
1
. Separating the vacuum
W^{\mu\nu}_{0}
from the in-medium
W^{\mu\nu}_{i}
contributions gives the following:

\displaystyle\frac{dW^{\mu\nu}_{0}}{dy}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{f}\int dxF^{A}_{{}_{f}}(x)\mathcal{H}^{\mu\nu}_{0}\mathcal{K}_{0},

\displaystyle\frac{dW^{\mu\nu}_{i}}{dy}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{f}\int dxF^{A}_{{}_{f}}(x)\mathcal{H}^{\mu\nu}_{0}\mathcal{K}_{i},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\mu\nu}_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{e^{2}_{f}}{2}(2\pi)\delta\left[\left(q+xP\right)^{2}\right]{\rm Tr}\left[\not{P}\gamma^{\mu}\left(\not{q}+x\not{P}\right)\gamma^{\nu}\right],

(5)

where the tensor
\mathcal{H}^{\mu\nu}_{0}
encodes the perturbative partonic contributions, with no emission and no in-medium scattering. The radiated gluon momentum fraction
y
is given as
y=\ell^{-}_{2}/q^{-}
, where the radiated final state gluon momemtum (
\ell_{2}
) is traveling in the negative
z
-direction and is collinear to the direction of the final-state quark. The parton distribution function (PDF), present in both vacuum and in-medium portions of
W^{\mu\nu}
, is defined as

\displaystyle F^{A}_{f}(x)=A\int\frac{dy^{-}}{2\pi}e^{-ixP^{+}y^{-}}\frac{1}{2}\left\langle P\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}\left(y^{-}\right)\gamma^{+}\psi_{{}_{f}}(0)\right|P\right\rangle,

(6)

and encodes the probability of finding a quark of flavor
f
, with momentum fraction
x
, in the nucleus
A
. The momentum fraction
x
carried by the struck quark is
x=p^{+}/P^{+}
, where
p^{+}
is the first component of the quark’s light-cone momentum, while
P^{+}
is the corresponding momentum component of the nucleon in the nucleus. The expectation value
\langle P|\bar{\psi}_{f}\left(y^{-}\right)\gamma^{+}\psi_{f}(0)|P\rangle
is a two-point fermionic correlator with a light-cone separation
y^{-}
along negative
z
-direction.
3
3

3

The quark spin and color averaging factors
1/(2N_{c})
are absorbed in the PDF.
The main objective of this contribution is to compute the kernels
\mathcal{K}_{i}
, where
\mathcal{K}_{0}
describes the vacuum kernel, while the different
\mathcal{K}_{i=1,2,3,4}
encode in-medium interactions described in Fig.
1
(a–d), respectively. Separate discussions of the hadronic tensor for the vacuum and medium-modified radiation are thus present.

II.1
Single gluon emission from the hard quark without in-medium scattering: the vacuum contribution

Figure 3
:
Forward scattering diagrams of leading order gluon production from the quark. The cut-line (i.e., dashed line) represents the final state.

Four diagrams contribute to single-gluon emission in vacuum. In the light-cone gauge
(A^{-}=0)
, Fig.
3
(a) represents the dominant, leading log, contribution, with the remaining three diagrams being subleading.
4
4

4

The subleading nature of diagrams in Fig.
3
(b–d) is analogous to photon production from an incoming virtual quark
Kumar and Vujanovic 2025
.
The hadronic tensor for the diagram shown in Fig.
3
(a) is given as

\displaystyle\begin{split}W^{\mu\nu}_{0,a}&=\sum_{f}2\left[-g^{\mu\nu}_{\perp\perp}\right]e^{2}_{f}\int d(\Delta X^{-})e^{iq^{+}\Delta X^{-}}\left\langle AP\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}\left(\Delta X^{-}\right)\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}(0)\right|AP\right\rangle\\
&\times g^{2}_{s}C_{F}C_{A}\int\frac{dy}{2\pi}\frac{d^{2}\ell_{2\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i\left\{\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}+yM^{2}}{2y\left(1-y\right)q^{-}}\right\}\Delta X^{-}}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\frac{[\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}+M^{2}y^{4}\kappa]}{\left[\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}+y^{2}M^{2}\right]^{2}},\end{split}

(7)

where
M
is the mass of the struck quark,
C_{A}=N_{c}=3
,
C_{F}=(N^{2}_{c}-1)/(2N_{c})=4/3
, while

\displaystyle\kappa=\frac{1}{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}},

(8)

also included in Appendix
A
for the reader’s convenience. The emitted gluon, carrying momentum
\ell_{2}
, is traveling in the negative
z
-direction, having momentum fraction
y=\ell^{-}_{2}/q^{-}
; in fact,
\ell^{\mu}_{2}=\left[\frac{\ell^{2}_{2\perp}}{2yq^{-}},yq^{-},\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}\right]
. Note that in Eq. (
2
) and Eq. (
7
), one factor of
e^{2}
has already been accounted for in the hadronic tensor, giving
\alpha^{2}_{em}
in Eq. (
2
).

Any calculation of
W^{\mu\nu}
proceeds by first obtaining the full
T
-matrix amplitude
T^{\mu\nu}
of a given process before extracting the forward scattering amplitude [Eq. (7.49) in Ref.
Peskin and Schroeder 1995
] using

\displaystyle W^{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\rm Disc}\left[T^{\mu\nu}\right].

(9)

The power counting of the small scale
\lambda
within
\ell_{2}
, reveals
\ell^{\mu}_{2}\sim\left[O(\lambda^{2}),O(1),O(\lambda),O(\lambda)\right]Q
. The outgoing quark’s momentum is given by
p^{\mu}_{2}=\left[\frac{\ell^{2}_{2\perp}+M^{2}}{2\left(1-y\right)q^{-}},\left(1-y\right)q^{-},-\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}\right]
, which scales as
p^{\mu}_{2}\sim[O(\lambda^{2}),O(1),O(\lambda),O(\lambda)]Q
and admits
p^{2}_{2}=M^{2}\geq 0
. Having established the vacuum result, including the relevant
\lambda
power counting, interactions with the medium are next considered.

II.2
Classification of single-scattering-induced parton radiation diagrams

In this section, we consider DIS between the virtual photon and the nucleus in which the struck quark, after hard scattering, undergoes various in-medium scatterings with the nuclear environment, allowing for multiple final states. The in-medium QCD scattering kernels for a quark contributing at
O(\alpha^{2}_{s})
are classified using the identity of final-state particles. The first kind of kernel (
\mathcal{K}_{1}
) contains a real gluon and a quark in the final state shown in Fig.
1
(a), and the gluon spectrum is modified in the nuclear medium via single Glauber-gluon
Idilbi and Majumder 2009
scattering herein. A total of eleven possible diagrams contribute to
\mathcal{K}_{1}
, which are shown in Fig.
4
. Section
III
presents the details of the calculation giving
\mathcal{K}_{1}
, for one of the diagrams. The remaining diagrams are in Appendix
B
.

The second kind of kernel
(\mathcal{K}_{2})
represents the two real gluons emission process
q\to g+g
where a Glauber quark is used to annihilate the incoming highly virtual quark, as depicted in Fig.
1
(b). There are a total of five possible central cut diagrams contributing to this kernel, shown in Fig.
6
, are discussed in Appendix
C
.

The third kind of kernel
(\mathcal{K}_{3})
represents both the
q\to q+\bar{q}^{\prime}
process where a Glauber antiquark is the scattering partner of the highly virtual incoming quark, as well as the
q\to q^{\prime}+\bar{q}^{\prime}
where a Glauber antiquark is used to annihilate the incoming highly-virtual quark. Both
q\to q+\bar{q}^{\prime}
and
q\to q^{\prime}+\bar{q}^{\prime}
possibilities are depicted in Fig.
7
, with four contributing central cut diagrams discussed in Appendix
D
.

The last kind of kernel
(\mathcal{K}_{4})
represents both the
q\to q+q^{\prime}
process where a Glauber quark is the scattering partner of the highly-virtual incoming quark. The two possible diagrams are depicted in Fig.
8
, while their hadronic tensor is calculated in Appendix
E
.

Of course, there is nothing special about our assumption of an incoming highly virtual quark. Our results also hold for an incoming highly virtual antiquark instead, with the corresponding change conjugation applied to the Glauber quark.

III
Single-Scattering induced emission: the one gluon and one quark final state

Figure 4
:
Diagrams for scattering kernel-1.

This section presents the calculation of all possible diagrams in which the hard quark produced in the primary hard scattering undergoes a single-gluon emission and single Glauber-gluon
Idilbi and Majumder 2009
exchange with the nuclear medium. The diagrams are referred to as kernel-1 and are depicted in Fig.
4
. There are a total of 23 diagrams. The calculation is performed in light-cone gauge
n\cdot A=A^{-}=0
, where light-cone vector
n=[1,0,0_{\perp}]
. The polarization tensor of the gluon propagator is given as

\displaystyle d^{(X)}_{\mu\nu}=-g_{\mu\nu}+\frac{X_{\mu}n_{\nu}+n_{\mu}X_{\nu}}{n\cdot X},

(10)

where
X
is the gluon’s momentum.

Presented below is the detailed calculation of the central-cut diagram in Fig.
4
(a). The remaining diagrams are calculated in Appendix
B
.

Figure 5
:
A forward scattering diagram in kernel-1.

The amplitude for the central-cut diagram in Fig.
5
is given as

\displaystyle\begin{split}T^{\mu\nu}_{1,c}&=\sum_{f}e^{2}_{f}g^{4}_{s}\int d^{4}x_{1}d^{4}x_{2}d^{4}z_{1}d^{4}z_{2}d^{4}z_{3}d^{4}z_{4}\int\frac{d^{4}p_{1}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}p^{\prime}_{1}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}\ell}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}\ell^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}\ell_{2}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}p_{2}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\\
&\times e^{ix_{2}\left(q-p^{\prime}_{1}\right)}e^{ix_{1}\left(p_{1}-q\right)}e^{iz_{1}(\ell+p_{2}-p_{1})}e^{iz_{2}(\ell_{2}-\ell)}e^{iz_{3}(\ell^{\prime}-\ell_{2})}e^{iz_{4}(p^{\prime}_{1}-\ell^{\prime}-p_{2})}\\
&\times\left\langle AP\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left[\psi_{{}_{f}}(x_{1})\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}(x_{2})\gamma^{\mu}\frac{i\left(\not{p}^{\prime}_{1}+M\right)}{\left[\left(p^{\prime}_{1}\right)^{2}-M^{2}+i\epsilon\right]}it^{a}\gamma^{\alpha_{1}}\frac{i\left(\not{p}_{2}+M\right)}{\left[p^{2}_{2}-M^{2}+i\epsilon\right]}it^{e}\gamma^{\alpha_{2}}\frac{i\left(\not{p}_{1}+M\right)}{\left[p^{2}_{1}-M^{2}+i\epsilon\right]}\gamma^{\nu}\right]\right|AP\right\rangle\\
&\times if^{abc}g^{\sigma_{1}\rho_{1}}\left(-\ell^{\prime}-\ell_{2}\right)^{\beta_{1}}A^{c}_{\beta_{1}}if^{bed}g^{\sigma_{2}\rho_{2}}\left(-\ell_{2}-\ell\right)^{\beta_{2}}A^{d}_{\beta_{2}}\frac{id^{(\ell)}_{\alpha_{2}\rho_{2}}}{\left(\ell^{2}+i\epsilon\right)}\frac{id^{(\ell_{2})}_{\sigma_{2}\rho_{1}}}{\left(\ell^{2}_{2}+i\epsilon\right)}\frac{id^{(\ell^{\prime})}_{\sigma_{1}\alpha_{1}}}{\left(\ell^{\prime 2}+i\epsilon\right)},\end{split}

(11)

where the trace Tr[…] is over the Dirac matrices and the Gell-Mann SU(3) color matrices. The notation
\sum_{f}
represents the sum over quark flavors. We apply Cutkosky’s
Cutkosky 1960
;
Peskin and Schroeder 1995
procedure to evaluate the discontinuity along the cut-line and obtain the hadronic tensor. The discontinuity associated with the gluon propagator and quark propagator is given as

\displaystyle\mathrm{Disc}\left[\frac{1}{\ell^{2}_{2}+i\epsilon}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\pi i\delta\left(\ell^{2}_{2}\right),

\displaystyle\mathrm{Disc}\left[\frac{1}{p^{2}_{2}-M^{2}+i\epsilon}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\pi i\delta(p^{2}_{2}-M^{2}).

(12)

The resulting hadronic tensor is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}W^{\mu\nu}_{1,c}&=\sum_{f}e^{2}_{f}g^{4}_{s}\int d^{4}x_{1}d^{4}x_{2}d^{4}z_{1}d^{4}z_{2}d^{4}z_{3}d^{4}z_{4}\int\frac{d^{4}p_{1}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}p^{\prime}_{1}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}\ell}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}\ell^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}\ell_{2}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}p_{2}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\\
&\times e^{ix_{2}\left(q-p^{\prime}_{1}\right)}e^{ix_{1}\left(p_{1}-q\right)}e^{iz_{1}(\ell+p_{2}-p_{1})}e^{iz_{2}(\ell_{2}-\ell)}e^{iz_{3}(\ell^{\prime}-\ell_{2})}e^{iz_{4}(p^{\prime}_{1}-\ell^{\prime}-p_{2})}\\
&\times\left\langle AP\left|\mathrm{Tr}\left[\psi_{{}_{f}}(x_{1})\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}(x_{2})\gamma^{\mu}\frac{(-i)\left(\not{p}^{\prime}_{1}+M\right)}{\left[\left(p^{\prime}_{1}\right)^{2}-M^{2}-i\epsilon\right]}(-i)t^{a}\gamma^{\alpha_{1}}\left(\not{p}_{2}+M\right)it^{e}\gamma^{\alpha_{2}}\frac{i\left(\not{p}_{1}+M\right)}{\left[p^{2}_{1}-M^{2}+i\epsilon\right]}\gamma^{\nu}\right]\right|AP\right\rangle\\
&\times if^{abc}g^{\sigma_{1}\rho_{1}}\left(\ell^{\prime}+\ell_{2}\right)^{\beta_{1}}A^{c}_{\beta_{1}}if^{bed}g^{\sigma_{2}\rho_{2}}\left(\ell_{2}+\ell\right)^{\beta_{2}}A^{d}_{\beta_{2}}\frac{id^{(\ell)}_{\alpha_{2}\rho_{2}}}{\left(\ell^{2}+i\epsilon\right)}d^{(\ell_{2})}_{\sigma_{2}\rho_{1}}\frac{(-i)d^{(\ell^{\prime})}_{\sigma_{1}\alpha_{1}}}{\left[\left(\ell^{\prime}\right)^{2}-i\epsilon\right]}(2\pi)\delta\left(p^{2}_{2}-M^{2}\right)(2\pi)\delta\left(\ell^{2}_{2}\right).\end{split}

(13)

To separate the perturbative and non-perturbative portions of this calculation, a power-counting scheme is established. The incoming quark before primary scattering is moving in a positive direction, i.e.,
p=\left[p^{+},M^{2}/2p^{+},\boldsymbol{0}_{\perp}\right]
, and thus
p^{\mu}\sim\left[O(1),O\left(\lambda\right),\boldsymbol{0}_{\perp}\right]Q
. The same
\lambda
-scales also hold for
p^{\prime}
since
p^{\prime+}/Q\sim 1
and
p^{\prime}=\left[p^{\prime+},M^{2}/2p^{\prime+},\boldsymbol{0}_{\perp}\right]
. Isolating the leading non-perturbative component, which is
\psi(x_{1})\bar{\psi}(x_{2})
for the first scattering correlator herein, allows to write
\psi(x_{1})\bar{\psi}(x_{2})
in terms of a scalar function
T(x_{1},x_{2})
:

\begin{split}&\psi(x_{1})\bar{\psi}(x_{2})=\not{p}T(x_{1},x_{2})=p^{+}\gamma^{-}T(x_{1},x_{2})\Longrightarrow{\rm Tr}[\gamma^{+}\psi(x_{1})\bar{\psi}(x_{2})]=p^{+}{\rm Tr}[\gamma^{+}\gamma^{-}]T(x_{1},x_{2})\\
&\Longrightarrow\psi(x_{1})\bar{\psi}(x_{2})=\gamma^{-}{\rm Tr}\left[\bar{\psi}(x_{2})\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi(x_{1})\right].\end{split}

(14)

In the light-cone gauge
A^{-}=0
, the Glauber gluon
Idilbi and Majumder 2009
emanating from the medium has
A^{+}\ggg A_{\perp}
, and thus
(\ell^{\prime}+\ell_{2})^{\beta_{1}}A^{c}_{\beta_{1}}(z_{3})\approx(\ell^{\prime-}+\ell_{2}^{-})A^{c+}(z_{3})
and
(\ell_{2}+\ell)^{\beta_{2}}A^{d}_{\beta_{2}}(z_{2})\approx(\ell^{-}_{2}+\ell^{-})A^{d+}(z_{2})
. In addition, we assume that the hard quark produced from the primary hard scattering with the nucleon struck by the virtual photon will undergo further rescatterings while traversing the remaining
A-1
nucleons. As in
Q\ggg 1
, any scatterings after the first one are assumed to be independent, and thus the correlators are factorized.

\left\langle AP\left|{\rm Tr}\left[\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}(x_{2})\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}(x_{1})\right]A^{c+}(z_{3})A^{d+}(z_{2})\right|AP\right\rangle\approx\left\langle P\left|{\rm Tr}\left[\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}\left(x_{2}\right)\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}\left(x_{1}\right)\right]\right|P\right\rangle\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|A^{c+}\left(z_{3}\right)A^{d+}\left(z_{2}\right)\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle,

(15)

where the first term will be absorbed in the definition of the nuclear parton distribution function, while the second term will be included in the scattering kernel.
The color trace can be simplified as

i^{2}f^{bed}f^{abc}{\rm Tr}[t^{a}t^{e}]=-\frac{f^{bad}f^{abc}}{2}=\frac{f^{dab}f^{cab}}{2}=C_{A}\frac{\delta^{dc}}{2}=C_{A}{\rm Tr}\left[t^{d}t^{c}\right],

(16)

where
C_{A}=N_{c}
. The resulting
W^{\mu\nu}
is given as

\displaystyle\begin{split}W^{\mu\nu}_{1,c}&=C_{A}\sum_{f}e^{2}_{f}g^{4}_{s}\int d^{4}x_{1}d^{4}x_{2}d^{4}z_{1}d^{4}z_{2}d^{4}z_{3}d^{4}z_{4}\int\frac{d^{4}p_{1}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}p^{\prime}_{1}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}\ell}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}\ell^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}\ell_{2}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}p_{2}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\left\langle P\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}(x_{2})\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}(x_{1})\right|P\right\rangle\\
&\times e^{ix_{2}\left(q-p^{\prime}_{1}\right)}e^{ix_{1}\left(p_{1}-q\right)}e^{iz_{1}(\ell+p_{2}-p_{1})}e^{iz_{2}(\ell_{2}-\ell)}e^{iz_{3}(\ell^{\prime}-\ell_{2})}e^{iz_{4}(p^{\prime}_{1}-\ell^{\prime}-p_{2})}\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|{\rm Tr}\left[A^{+}\left(z_{3}\right)A^{+}\left(z_{2}\right)\right]\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle\\
&\times\mathrm{Tr}\left[\gamma^{-}\gamma^{\mu}\frac{\left(\not{p}^{\prime}_{1}+M\right)}{\left[\left(p^{\prime}_{1}\right)^{2}-M^{2}-i\epsilon\right]}\gamma^{\alpha_{1}}\left(\not{p}_{2}+M\right)\gamma^{\alpha_{2}}\frac{\left(\not{p}_{1}+M\right)}{\left[p^{2}_{1}-M^{2}+i\epsilon\right]}\gamma^{\nu}\right]\\
&\times g^{\sigma_{1}\rho_{1}}\left(\ell^{\prime-}+\ell_{2}^{-}\right)g^{\sigma_{2}\rho_{2}}\left(\ell^{-}_{2}+\ell^{-}\right)\frac{d^{(\ell)}_{\alpha_{2}\rho_{2}}}{\left(\ell^{2}+i\epsilon\right)}d^{(\ell_{2})}_{\sigma_{2}\rho_{1}}\frac{d^{(\ell^{\prime})}_{\sigma_{1}\alpha_{1}}}{\left[\left(\ell^{\prime}\right)^{2}-i\epsilon\right]}(2\pi)\delta\left(p^{2}_{2}-M^{2}\right)(2\pi)\delta\left(\ell^{2}_{2}\right).\end{split}

(17)

After performing the change of variable
p^{\prime}_{1}=q+p^{\prime}
and
p_{1}=q+p
, which stems from energy and momentum conservation (see Fig.
5
), the integration measure
d^{4}p^{\prime}_{1}
transforms as
d^{4}p^{\prime}_{1}\to d^{4}p^{\prime}
, while
d^{4}p_{1}\to d^{4}p
.
We also perform the integrals over
d^{4}z_{1}
and
d^{4}z_{4}
gives

(2\pi)^{4}\delta^{(4)}\left(-q-p+\ell+p_{2}\right)(2\pi)^{4}\delta^{(4)}\left(q+p^{\prime}-\ell^{\prime}-p_{2}\right)
,
which allows for the
d^{4}\ell
and
d^{4}\ell^{\prime}
integration. The
W^{\mu\nu}_{1,c}
becomes

\displaystyle\begin{split}W^{\mu\nu}_{1,c}&=C_{A}\sum_{f}e^{2}_{f}g^{4}_{s}\int d^{4}x_{1}d^{4}x_{2}d^{4}z_{2}d^{4}z_{3}\int\frac{d^{4}p}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}p^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}\ell_{2}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}p_{2}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\left\langle P\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}(x_{2})\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}(x_{1})\right|P\right\rangle\\
&\times e^{-ix_{2}p^{\prime}}e^{ix_{1}p}e^{iz_{2}(\ell_{2}+p_{2}-q-p)}e^{iz_{3}(q+p^{\prime}-p_{2}-\ell_{2})}\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|{\rm Tr}\left[A^{+}\left(z_{3}\right)A^{+}\left(z_{2}\right)\right]\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle\\
&\times\mathrm{Tr}\left[\gamma^{-}\gamma^{\mu}\frac{\left(\not{q}+\not{p}^{\prime}+M\right)}{\left[\left(q+p^{\prime}\right)^{2}-M^{2}-i\epsilon\right]}\gamma^{\alpha_{1}}\left(\not{p}_{2}+M\right)\gamma^{\alpha_{2}}\frac{\left(\not{q}+\not{p}+M\right)}{\left[\left(q+p\right)^{2}-M^{2}+i\epsilon\right]}\gamma^{\nu}\right]\\
&\times g^{\sigma_{1}\rho_{1}}g^{\sigma_{2}\rho_{2}}\left(q^{-}-p^{-}_{2}+\ell_{2}^{-}\right)^{2}\frac{d^{\left(q+p-p_{2}\right)}_{\alpha_{2}\rho_{2}}}{\left[\left(q+p-p_{2}\right)^{2}+i\epsilon\right]}d^{\left(\ell_{2}\right)}_{\sigma_{2}\rho_{1}}\frac{d^{\left(q+p^{\prime}-p_{2}\right)}_{\sigma_{1}\alpha_{1}}}{\left[\left(q+p^{\prime}-p_{2}\right)^{2}-i\epsilon\right]}(2\pi)\delta\left(p^{2}_{2}-M^{2}\right)(2\pi)\delta\left(\ell^{2}_{2}\right).\end{split}

(18)

Introducing new variables for distances

\displaystyle\Delta x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x_{2}-x_{1},\quad x=\frac{x_{2}+x_{1}}{2},

\displaystyle\Delta z

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle z_{3}-z_{2},\quad z=\frac{z_{3}+z_{2}}{2},

(19)

leaves the integration measure unchanged, namely
d^{4}x_{2}d^{4}x_{1}=d^{4}xd^{4}(\Delta x)
and
d^{4}z_{3}d^{4}z_{2}=d^{4}zd^{4}(\Delta z)
. Moreover, that the two-point correlator
\langle P|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}(x+\Delta x/2)\gamma^{+}\psi_{{}_{f}}(x-\Delta x/2)|P\rangle
is invariant under translation by four-vector
x
. This is primarily true owing to the fact that the incoming state and the outgoing state are identical. Thus, the hadronic tensor becomes

\displaystyle\begin{split}W^{\mu\nu}_{1,c}&=C_{A}\sum_{f}e^{2}_{f}g^{4}_{s}\int d^{4}xd^{4}(\Delta x)d^{4}zd^{4}(\Delta z)\int\frac{d^{4}p}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}p^{\prime}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}\ell_{2}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}p_{2}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\left\langle P\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}\left(\Delta x\right)\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}\left(0\right)\right|P\right\rangle\\
&\times e^{-i\left(x+\frac{\Delta x}{2}\right)p^{\prime}}e^{i\left(x-\frac{\Delta x}{2}\right)p}e^{i\left(z-\frac{\Delta z}{2}\right)\left(\ell_{2}+p_{2}-q-p\right)}e^{i\left(z+\frac{\Delta z}{2}\right)\left(q+p^{\prime}-p_{2}-\ell_{2}\right)}\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|{\rm Tr}\left[A^{+}\left(z+\frac{\Delta z}{2}\right)A^{+}\left(z-\frac{\Delta z}{2}\right)\right]\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle\\
&\times\mathrm{Tr}\left[\gamma^{-}\gamma^{\mu}\frac{\left(\not{q}+\not{p}^{\prime}+M\right)}{\left[\left(q+p^{\prime}\right)^{2}-M^{2}-i\epsilon\right]}\gamma^{\alpha_{1}}\left(\not{p}_{2}+M\right)\gamma^{\alpha_{2}}\frac{\left(\not{q}+\not{p}+M\right)}{\left[\left(q+p\right)^{2}-M^{2}+i\epsilon\right]}\gamma^{\nu}\right]\\
&\times g^{\sigma_{1}\rho_{1}}g^{\sigma_{2}\rho_{2}}\left(q^{-}-p^{-}_{2}+\ell_{2}^{-}\right)^{2}\frac{d^{\left(q+p-p_{2}\right)}_{\alpha_{2}\rho_{2}}}{\left[\left(q+p-p_{2}\right)^{2}+i\epsilon\right]}d^{\left(\ell_{2}\right)}_{\sigma_{2}\rho_{1}}\frac{d^{\left(q+p^{\prime}-p_{2}\right)}_{\sigma_{1}\alpha_{1}}}{\left[\left(q+p^{\prime}-p_{2}\right)^{2}-i\epsilon\right]}(2\pi)\delta\left(p^{2}_{2}-M^{2}\right)(2\pi)\delta\left(\ell^{2}_{2}\right).\end{split}

(20)

Equation (
20
) becomes singular when the denominator of the propagator for
p_{1}
,
\ell
,
\ell^{\prime}
and
p^{\prime}_{1}
vanishes. Computing this integral is easiest in the complex plane of
p^{+}
and
p^{\prime+}
, where both
p^{+}
and
p^{\prime+}
have two simple poles.
5
5

5

One of the propagators takes the form

\displaystyle\left[\left(q+p\right)^{2}-M^{2}+i\epsilon\right]^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[2\left(q^{+}+p^{+}\right)\left(q^{-}+p^{-}\right)-\left|\boldsymbol{q}_{\perp}+\boldsymbol{p}_{\perp}\right|^{2}-M^{2}+i\epsilon\right]^{-1}\approx\left[2q^{-}\left(q^{+}+p^{+}\right)\left[1+O\left(\lambda^{2}\right)\right]-M^{2}+i\epsilon\right]^{-1}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow\left[\left(q+p\right)^{2}-M^{2}+i\epsilon\right]^{-1}

\displaystyle\approx

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2q^{-}}\left[q^{+}+p^{+}-\frac{M^{2}}{2q^{-}}+i\epsilon\right]^{-1}

(21)

where the established power counting
p^{-}/q^{-}\sim\lambda^{2}
together with
\boldsymbol{p}_{\perp}=\boldsymbol{0}_{\perp}
was used to simplify the full propagator to the expression above. A similar procedure is used for
\left[\left(q+p-p_{2}\right)^{2}+i\epsilon\right]
.

The contour integration for
p^{+}
can be carried out as

\begin{split}C_{1}&=\oint\frac{dp^{+}}{(2\pi)}\frac{e^{ip^{+}(x^{-}_{1}-z^{-}_{2})}}{\left[\left(q+p\right)^{2}-M^{2}+i\epsilon\right]\left[\left(q+p-p_{2}\right)^{2}+i\epsilon\right]}\\
&=\oint\frac{dp^{+}}{(2\pi)}\frac{e^{ip^{+}(x^{-}_{1}-z^{-}_{2})}}{2q^{-}\left[q^{+}+p^{+}-\frac{M^{2}}{2q^{-}}+i\epsilon\right]2\left(q^{-}-p^{-}_{2}\right)\left[q^{+}+p^{+}-p^{+}_{2}-\frac{\boldsymbol{p}^{2}_{2\perp}}{2(q^{-}-p^{-}_{2})}+i\epsilon\right]}\\
&=\frac{(2\pi i)}{2\pi}\frac{\theta(x^{-}_{1}-z^{-}_{2})}{4q^{-}(q^{-}-p^{-}_{2})}\left[\frac{e^{i\left(-q^{+}+\frac{M^{2}}{2q^{-}}\right)\left(x^{-}_{1}-z^{-}_{2}\right)}}{\left\{\frac{M^{2}}{2q^{-}}-p^{+}_{2}-\frac{\boldsymbol{p}^{2}_{2\perp}}{2(q^{-}-p^{-}_{2})}\right\}}+\frac{e^{i\left(-q^{+}+p^{+}_{2}+\frac{\boldsymbol{p}^{2}_{2\perp}}{2\left(q^{-}-p^{-}_{2}\right)}\right)\left(x^{-}_{1}-z^{-}_{2}\right)}}{\left\{p^{+}_{2}+\frac{\boldsymbol{p}^{2}_{2\perp}}{2\left(q^{-}-p^{-}_{2}\right)}-\frac{M^{2}}{2q^{-}}\right\}}\right]\\
&=\frac{(2\pi i)}{2\pi}\frac{\theta(x^{-}_{1}-z^{-}_{2})}{4q^{-}(q^{-}-p^{-}_{2})}e^{i\left(-q^{+}+\frac{M^{2}}{2q^{-}}\right)\left(x^{-}_{1}-z^{-}_{2}\right)}\left[\frac{-1+e^{i\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}(x^{-}_{1}-z^{-}_{2})}}{\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}}\right],\end{split}

(22)

where

\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}=p^{+}_{2}+\frac{\boldsymbol{p}^{2}_{2\perp}}{2(q^{-}-p^{-}_{2})}-\frac{M^{2}}{2q^{-}},

(23)

which is also present in Appendix
A
for convenience. The contour integration for
p^{\prime+}
proceeds analogously, giving

\begin{split}C_{2}&=\oint\frac{dp^{\prime+}}{(2\pi)}\frac{e^{-ip^{\prime+}(x^{-}_{2}-z^{-}_{3})}}{\left[\left(q+p^{\prime}\right)^{2}-M^{2}-i\epsilon\right]\left[\left(q+p^{\prime}-p_{2}\right)^{2}-i\epsilon\right]}\\
&=\oint\frac{dp^{\prime+}}{(2\pi)}\frac{e^{-ip^{\prime+}(x^{-}_{2}-z^{-}_{3})}}{2q^{-}\left[q^{+}+p^{\prime+}-\frac{M^{2}}{2q^{-}}-i\epsilon\right]2(q^{-}-p^{-}_{2})\left[q^{+}+p^{\prime+}-p^{+}_{2}-\frac{\boldsymbol{p}^{2}_{2\perp}}{2(q^{-}-p^{-}_{2})}-i\epsilon\right]}\\
&=\frac{(-2\pi i)}{2\pi}\frac{\theta\left(x^{-}_{2}-z^{-}_{3}\right)}{4q^{-}(q^{-}-p^{-}_{2})}e^{-i\left(-q^{+}+\frac{M^{2}}{2q^{-}}\right)\left(x^{-}_{2}-z^{-}_{3}\right)}\left[\frac{-1+e^{-i\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}\left(x^{-}_{2}-z^{-}_{3}\right)}}{\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}}\right].\end{split}

(24)

As the final expression for
C_{1}
and
C_{2}
is independent of
p
and
p^{\prime}
, respectively, the dependence on these variables in Eq. (
20
) remains within
e^{ip(x_{1}-z_{2})}
and
e^{ip^{\prime}(z_{3}-x_{2})}
as well as the trace over
\gamma
-matrices. While our
\lambda
-power counting scheme constrains the size of momentum variables, the same cannot be said about position variables. Thus, the
e^{ip(x_{1}-z_{2})}
and
e^{ip^{\prime}(z_{3}-x_{2})}
phase factors must remain intact. As the trace in Eq. (
20
) only contributes at
O(\lambda^{2})
in
p
and
p^{\prime}
, the only non-trivial contribution remaining to the
p
and
p^{\prime}
integrals stems solely from
e^{ip(x_{1}-z_{2})}
and
e^{ip^{\prime}(z_{3}-x_{2})}
phase factors. To perform the remaining integrals for
p
and
p^{\prime}
, the following substitutions are used
p=\left[p^{+},p^{-},\boldsymbol{0}_{\perp}\right]=\left[p^{+},\frac{M^{2}}{2p^{+}}+\delta p^{-},\boldsymbol{0}_{\perp}\right]
and
p^{\prime}=\left[p^{\prime+},p^{\prime-},\boldsymbol{0}_{\perp}\right]=\left[p^{\prime+},\frac{M^{2}}{2p^{\prime+}}+\delta p^{\prime-},\boldsymbol{0}_{\perp}\right]
, where
\delta p^{-}\sim O\left(\lambda^{2}\right)
and
\delta p^{\prime-}\sim O\left(\lambda^{2}\right)
. Thus, the integrals over
dp^{-}d^{2}p_{\perp}dp^{\prime-}d^{2}p^{\prime}_{\perp}
simply become integrals over
d(\delta p^{-})d^{2}p_{\perp}d(\delta p^{\prime-})d^{2}p^{\prime}_{\perp}
yielding

\displaystyle(2\pi)^{6}\delta\left(x^{+}-\frac{\Delta x^{+}}{2}-z^{+}+\frac{\Delta z^{+}}{2}\right)\delta^{(2)}\left(\boldsymbol{x}_{\perp}-\frac{\Delta\boldsymbol{x}_{\perp}}{2}-\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}+\frac{\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}}{2}\right)\times

\displaystyle\times\delta\left(-x^{+}-\frac{\Delta x^{+}}{2}+z^{+}+\frac{\Delta z^{+}}{2}\right)\delta^{(2)}\left(-\boldsymbol{x}_{\perp}-\frac{\Delta\boldsymbol{x}_{\perp}}{2}+\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}+\frac{\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}}{2}\right).

(25)

Performing the integral over spacetime variables
(x^{+},\boldsymbol{x}_{\perp})
and
(\Delta x^{+},\Delta\boldsymbol{x}_{\perp})
using
\delta
-functions in Eq. (
25
) yields

\displaystyle\begin{split}W^{\mu\nu}_{1,c}&=C_{A}\sum_{f}e^{2}_{f}g^{4}_{s}\int dx^{-}d(\Delta x^{-})d^{4}zd^{4}(\Delta z)\int\frac{d^{4}\ell_{2}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\frac{d^{4}p_{2}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\left\langle P\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}\left(\Delta x^{-},\Delta z^{+},\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}\right)\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}\left(0\right)\right|P\right\rangle\\
&\times e^{i\left(q^{+}-\frac{M^{2}}{2q^{-}}\right)\left(\Delta x^{-}-\Delta z^{-}\right)}e^{i\Delta z(q-p_{2}-\ell_{2})}\left[-1+e^{i\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}\left(x^{-}-z^{-}-\frac{\Delta x^{-}}{2}+\frac{\Delta z^{-}}{2}\right)}\right]\left[-1+e^{-i\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}\left(x^{-}-z^{-}+\frac{\Delta x^{-}}{2}-\frac{\Delta z^{-}}{2}\right)}\right]\\
&\times\theta\left(x^{-}-z^{-}-\frac{\Delta x^{-}}{2}+\frac{\Delta z^{-}}{2}\right)\theta\left(x^{-}-z^{-}+\frac{\Delta x^{-}}{2}-\frac{\Delta z^{-}}{2}\right)\frac{1}{\left[4q^{-}\left(q^{-}-p^{-}_{2}\right)\right]^{2}}\left[\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}\right]^{-2}\\
&\times\mathrm{Tr}\left[\gamma^{-}\gamma^{\mu}\left(\not{q}+\not{p}^{\prime}+M\right)\gamma^{\alpha_{1}}\left(\not{p}_{2}+M\right)\gamma^{\alpha_{2}}\left(\not{q}+\not{p}+M\right)\gamma^{\nu}\right]\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|{\rm Tr}\left[A^{+}\left(z+\Delta z/2\right)A^{+}\left(z-\Delta z/2\right)\right]\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle\\
&\times g^{\sigma_{1}\rho_{1}}g^{\sigma_{2}\rho_{2}}\left(q^{-}-p^{-}_{2}+\ell_{2}^{-}\right)^{2}d^{(q+p-p_{2})}_{\alpha_{2}\rho_{2}}d^{(\ell_{2})}_{\sigma_{2}\rho_{1}}d^{(q+p^{\prime}-p_{2})}_{\sigma_{1}\alpha_{1}}(2\pi)\delta\left(p^{2}_{2}-M^{2}\right)(2\pi)\delta\left(\ell^{2}_{2}\right).\end{split}

(26)

The next step is to perform
d\ell^{+}_{2}
and
dp^{+}_{2}
using the
\delta
-functions
\delta(\ell^{2}_{2})
and
\delta(p^{2}_{2}-M^{2})
and
\lambda
-power counting. Indeed

\displaystyle\delta\left(\ell^{2}_{2}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta\left(2\ell^{+}_{2}\ell^{-}_{2}-\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}\right)=\frac{1}{2\ell^{-}_{2}}\delta\left(\ell^{+}_{2}-\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}{2\ell^{-}_{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\delta(p^{2}_{2}-M^{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\delta\left(2p^{+}_{2}p^{-}_{2}-\boldsymbol{p}^{2}_{2\perp}-M^{2}\right)=\frac{1}{2p^{-}_{2}}\delta\left(p^{+}_{2}-\frac{\boldsymbol{p}^{2}_{2\perp}+M^{2}}{2p^{-}_{2}}\right).

(27)

Defining the momentum fraction
y
as
\ell^{-}_{2}=yq^{-}
, allows to rewrite
d\ell^{-}_{2}=q^{-}dy
. Furthermore, energy and momentum conservation in Fig.
5
implies that

q+p=p_{1}=\ell_{2}+\ell=\ell_{2}+(p_{2}-k)\Longleftrightarrow q+p-\ell_{2}-p_{2}+k=0.

(28)

While the
\delta
-functions can be used to perform the
\ell^{+}_{2}
and
p^{+}_{2}
integrals, the
p^{-}_{2}
integral can be performed using
\lambda
-power counting. Indeed, as
k^{\mu}\sim\left[O\left(\lambda^{2}\right),O\left(\lambda^{2}\right),O(\lambda),O(\lambda)\right]Q
, while
\ell^{\mu}_{2}\sim\left[O\left(\lambda^{2}\right),O(1),O(\lambda),O(\lambda)\right]Q
and
p^{\mu}_{2}\sim\left[O\left(\lambda^{2}\right),O(1),O(\lambda),O(\lambda)\right]Q
, using energy and momentum conservation implies

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{-}+p^{-}-\ell^{-}_{2}-p^{-}_{2}+k^{-},

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle q^{-}+O\left(\lambda^{2}\right)-\ell^{-}_{2}-p^{-}_{2}+O(\lambda),

(29)

and thus the following change of variable
p^{-}_{2}=q^{-}-\ell^{-}_{2}+k^{-}+\delta p^{-}_{2}
, where
\delta p^{-}_{2}\sim O\left(\lambda^{2}\right)
is a small quantity, induces a change in the integration measure
dp^{-}_{2}=d\left(\delta p^{-}_{2}\right)
. Thus, the integration over
dp^{-}_{2}
yields a
\delta(\Delta z^{+})
, as the only function in Eq. (
26
) that is not small is
e^{-ip^{-}_{2}(\Delta z^{+})}
, since
\Delta z^{+}
is not subjet to the power counting in
\lambda
. Any other dependence on
p^{-}_{2}
seen in Eq. (
26
) can simply be set to
q^{-}-\ell^{-}_{2}+k^{-}
. Defining

\displaystyle\eta=\frac{k^{-}}{yq^{-}}

(30)

(see also Appendix
A
) and applying the following transformation
\boldsymbol{p}_{2\perp}+\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}=\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}
, allows to express
d^{2}p_{2\perp}\to d^{2}k_{2\perp}
, for a fixed
\ell_{2}
, thus giving

\displaystyle\begin{split}W^{\mu\nu}_{1,c}&=C_{A}\sum_{f}e^{2}_{f}g^{4}_{s}\int dx^{-}d(\Delta x^{-})d^{4}zd^{4}(\Delta z)\int\frac{dyd^{2}\ell_{2\perp}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{d^{2}k_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\delta\left(\Delta z^{+}\right)e^{-i\Delta z^{+}k^{-}}\left\langle P\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}\left(\Delta x^{-},\Delta z^{+},\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}\right)\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}\left(0\right)\right|P\right\rangle\\
&\times e^{i\left(q^{+}-\frac{M^{2}}{2q^{-}}\right)\Delta x^{-}}\left[-1+e^{i\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}\left(x^{-}-z^{-}-\frac{\Delta x^{-}}{2}+\frac{\Delta z^{-}}{2}\right)}\right]\left[-1+e^{-i\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}\left(x^{-}-z^{-}+\frac{\Delta x^{-}}{2}-\frac{\Delta z^{-}}{2}\right)}\right]e^{-i\Delta z^{-}\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{M}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\cdot\boldsymbol{\Delta z}_{\perp}}\\
&\times\theta\left(x^{-}-z^{-}-\frac{\Delta x^{-}}{2}+\frac{\Delta z^{-}}{2}\right)\theta\left(x^{-}-z^{-}+\frac{\Delta x^{-}}{2}-\frac{\Delta z^{-}}{2}\right)\frac{1}{\left[4q^{-}\left(q^{-}-p^{-}_{2}\right)\right]^{2}}\left[\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}\right]^{-2}\frac{q^{-}}{2yq^{-}2\left(1-y+\eta y\right)q^{-}}\\
&\times\mathrm{Tr}\left[\gamma^{-}\gamma^{\mu}\left(\not{q}+\not{p}^{\prime}+M\right)\gamma^{\alpha_{1}}\left(\not{p}_{2}+M\right)\gamma^{\alpha_{2}}\left(\not{q}+\not{p}+M\right)\gamma^{\nu}\right]\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|{\rm Tr}\left[A^{+}\left(z+\Delta z/2\right)A^{+}\left(z-\Delta z/2\right)\right]\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle\\
&\times g^{\sigma_{1}\rho_{1}}g^{\sigma_{2}\rho_{2}}\left(2yq^{-}-k^{-}\right)^{2}d^{\left(q+p-p_{2}\right)}_{\alpha_{2}\rho_{2}}d^{\left(\ell_{2}\right)}_{\sigma_{2}\rho_{1}}d^{\left(q+p^{\prime}-p_{2}\right)}_{\sigma_{1}\alpha_{1}}.\end{split}

(31)

Note that the two-point gauge field operator
\langle P_{A-1}|{\rm Tr}[A^{+}(z+\Delta z/2)A^{+}(z-\Delta z/2)]|P_{A-1}\rangle
is invariant under translation by four-vector
z
. This is primarily true owing to the fact that the incoming state
|P_{A-1}\rangle
and the outgoing state
\langle P_{A-1}|
are identical. Therefore, any
z
dependence seen in the operator expectation value is not physical. The phases that depend on the relative distances
\Delta x^{-}=x^{-}_{2}-x^{-}_{1}
, such as
e^{i\left(q^{+}-\frac{M^{2}}{2q^{-}}\right)\Delta x^{-}}
, are absorbed in the definition of the quark PDF, while the phases
e^{-i\Delta z^{-}\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{M}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\cdot\boldsymbol{\Delta z}_{\perp}}
are included within the distribution function of the nuclear medium.

Since the process (Fig.
5
) in amplitude is identical to the process in the complex conjugate,
W^{\mu\nu}
must be a real number. Therefore, the remaining phase factors must be real-valued

\begin{split}&\mathcal{R}=\left[-1+e^{i\mathcal{G}^{\left(p_{2}\right)}_{M}\left(x^{-}-z^{-}-\frac{\Delta x^{-}}{2}+\frac{\Delta z^{-}}{2}\right)}\right]\left[-1+e^{-i\mathcal{G}^{\left(p_{2}\right)}_{M}\left(x^{-}-z^{-}+\frac{\Delta x^{-}}{2}-\frac{\Delta z^{-}}{2}\right)}\right]\\
&\mathcal{R}=\left[-1+e^{i\mathcal{G}^{\left(p_{2}\right)}_{M}\left(x^{-}_{1}-z^{-}_{2}\right)}\right]\left[-1+e^{-i\mathcal{G}^{\left(p_{2}\right)}_{M}\left(x^{-}_{2}-z^{-}_{3}\right)}\right]\\
&\mathcal{R}=\left[1-e^{i\mathcal{G}^{\left(p_{2}\right)}_{M}\left(x^{-}_{1}-z^{-}_{2}\right)}-e^{-i\mathcal{G}^{\left(p_{2}\right)}_{M}\left(x^{-}_{2}-z^{-}_{3}\right)}+e^{i\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}\left(x^{-}_{1}-z^{-}_{2}-x^{-}_{2}+z^{-}_{3}\right)}\right]\in\mathbb{R}\\
&\Longrightarrow\mathcal{G}^{\left(p_{2}\right)}_{M}(x^{-}_{1}-z^{-}_{2}-x^{-}_{2}+z^{-}_{3})=2n\pi,\text{ where, }n\in\mathbb{Z}\\
&\Longrightarrow\mathcal{G}^{\left(p_{2}\right)}_{M}(x^{-}_{1}-z^{-}_{2})=\mathcal{G}^{\left(p_{2}\right)}_{M}\left(x^{-}_{2}-z^{-}_{3}\right)+2n\pi,\\
&\Longrightarrow\mathcal{R}=\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{\left(p_{2}\right)}_{M}\left(x^{-}_{2}-z^{-}_{3}\right)\right\}\right]=\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{\left(p_{2}\right)}_{M}\left(x^{-}_{1}-z^{-}_{2}\right)\right\}\right].\end{split}

(32)

The above derivation entails that
x^{-}_{2}-z^{-}_{3}=x^{-}_{1}-z^{-}_{2}
, which is expected as
x^{-}_{1}-z^{-}_{2}
represents the distance between first scattering and second scattering on the amplitude side, while
x^{-}_{2}-z^{-}_{3}
is the same distance on the complex-conjugate side. As
\theta(x^{-}_{1}-z^{-}_{2})
suggests that
x^{-}_{1}-z^{-}_{2}>0
, while
\theta(x^{-}_{2}-z^{-}_{3})
implies
x^{-}_{2}-z^{-}_{3}>0
, a new length integration variable

\displaystyle\zeta^{-}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle x^{-}_{2}-z^{-}_{3}=x^{-}_{1}-z^{-}_{2}=x^{-}-z^{-},

(33)

is defined to encapsulate that spacetime distance, and ensure that the scattering probability is real-valued. Introducing
\zeta^{-}
in
W^{\mu\nu}
, allows to perform the integrals over
d^{4}z
,
6
6

6

The integral over
d^{4}z
gives an overall normalization factor, which is absorbed in the redefinition of the operator product expectation value.
and
\Delta z^{+}
, giving

\displaystyle\begin{split}W^{\mu\nu}_{1,c}&=C_{A}\sum_{f}e^{2}_{f}g^{4}_{s}\int d\zeta^{-}d(\Delta x^{-})d(\Delta z)d^{2}(\Delta z_{\perp})\int\frac{dyd^{2}\ell_{2\perp}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\frac{d^{2}k_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{i\left(q^{+}-\frac{M^{2}}{2q^{-}}\right)\Delta x^{-}}\left\langle P\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}\left(\Delta x^{-}\right)\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}\left(0\right)\right|P\right\rangle\\
&\times\theta\left(\zeta^{-}\right)\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\left[4q^{-}\left(1-\eta\right)yq^{-}\right]^{2}}\left[\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}\right]^{-2}\frac{q^{-}}{2yq^{-}2(1-y+\eta y)q^{-}}e^{-i\Delta z^{-}\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{M}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\cdot\boldsymbol{\Delta z}_{\perp}}\\
&\times\mathrm{Tr}\left[\gamma^{-}\gamma^{\mu}\left(\not{q}+\not{p}^{\prime}+M\right)\gamma^{\alpha_{1}}\left(\not{p}_{2}+M\right)\gamma^{\alpha_{2}}\left(\not{q}+\not{p}+M\right)\gamma^{\nu}\right]\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|{\rm Tr}\left[A^{+}\left(\zeta^{-},\Delta z^{-},\Delta z_{\perp}\right)A^{+}\left(\zeta^{-},0\right)\right]\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle\\
&\times g^{\sigma_{1}\rho_{1}}g^{\sigma_{2}\rho_{2}}\left(2yq^{-}-\eta yq^{-}\right)^{2}d^{(q+p-p_{2})}_{\alpha_{2}\rho_{2}}d^{(\ell_{2})}_{\sigma_{2}\rho_{1}}d^{(q+p^{\prime}-p_{2})}_{\sigma_{1}\alpha_{1}}.\end{split}

(34)

The trace in Eq. (
34
) can be simplified to get

\begin{split}&\mathrm{Tr}\left[\gamma^{-}\gamma^{\mu}\left(\not{q}+\not{p}^{\prime}+M\right)\gamma^{\alpha_{1}}\left(\not{p}_{2}+M\right)\gamma^{\alpha_{2}}\left(\not{q}+\not{p}+M\right)\gamma^{\nu}\right]g^{\sigma_{1}\rho_{1}}g^{\sigma_{2}\rho_{2}}d^{\left(q+p-p_{2}\right)}_{\alpha_{2}\rho_{2}}d^{\left(\ell_{2}\right)}_{\sigma_{2}\rho_{1}}d^{\left(q+p^{\prime}-p_{2}\right)}_{\sigma_{1}\alpha_{1}}\\
&=\frac{8q^{-}\left[-g^{\mu\nu}_{\perp\perp}\right]}{\left(1-\eta\right)^{2}y\left(1-y+\eta y\right)}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)^{2}+\kappa y^{4}M^{2}\right],\end{split}

(35)

where
\kappa
is defined in Eq. (
8
). Using the expression in Eq. (
35
), the hadronic tensor becomes

\displaystyle\begin{split}W^{\mu\nu}_{1,c}&=C_{A}\sum_{f}2\left[-g^{\mu\nu}_{\perp\perp}\right]e^{2}_{f}g^{4}_{s}\int d(\Delta x^{-})e^{i\Delta x^{-}\left(q^{+}-\frac{M^{2}}{2q^{-}}\right)}\left\langle P\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}(\Delta x^{-})\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}(0)\right|P\right\rangle\\
&\times\int d(\Delta z^{-})d^{2}\Delta z_{\perp}\frac{dy}{2\pi}\frac{d^{2}\ell_{2\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{d^{2}k_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\left[\frac{\left(1-\frac{\eta}{2}\right)^{2}}{\left(1-\eta\right)^{2}}\right]e^{-i\Delta z^{-}\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{M}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}}\\
&\times\int d\zeta^{-}\theta\left(\zeta^{-}\right)\frac{\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)^{2}+\kappa y^{4}M^{2}\right]\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)^{2}+y^{2}\left(1-\eta\right)^{2}M^{2}\right]^{2}}\\
&\times\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|{\rm Tr}\left[A^{+}\left(\zeta^{-},\Delta z^{-},\Delta z_{\perp}\right)A^{+}\left(\zeta^{-},0\right)\right]\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle,\end{split}

(36)

where, for completeness,

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p^{+}_{2}+\frac{\boldsymbol{p}^{2}_{2\perp}}{2(q^{-}-p^{-}_{2})}-\frac{M^{2}}{2q^{-}}=\frac{\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)^{2}+y^{2}\left(1-\eta\right)^{2}M^{2}}{2y\left(1-y+\eta y\right)\left(1-\eta\right)q^{-}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{M}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ell^{+}_{2}+p^{+}_{2}-\frac{M^{2}}{2q^{-}}=\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}-yM^{2}}{2yq^{-}}+\frac{\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)^{2}+M^{2}}{2q^{-}\left(1-y+\eta y\right)}.

(37)

Note that momentum variables, such as
\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}
and
\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{M}
are in Appendix
A
for the reader’s convenience.

IV
Full scattering kernel at next-to-leading order (NLO) and next-to-leading twist (NLT)

In the preceding sections, the goal was to present in detail the steps involved in the derivation of a hadronic tensor for a given diagram and to highlight the power-counting strategy employed herein. In this section, for each kernel, all diagrams are combined and a full scattering kernel is provided for the gluonic and fermionic emissions from quark-initiated jet-medium interactions in each category:
q\to q+g
,
q\to g+g
,
q\to q+\bar{q}^{\prime}
, and
q\to q^{\prime}+\bar{q}^{\prime}
. The
q\to q+g
case is analysed first, which involves a Glauber gluon
Idilbi and Majumder 2009
exchange with the medium, while all other diagrams subsequently explored involve a Glauber quark exchange with the medium. Kernels involving Glauber quarks are especially interesting, as quark degrees of freedom develop dynamically as the heavy-ion collision transitions from a glasma-like initial state to the QGP. So, kernels
\mathcal{K}_{2}
through
\mathcal{K}_{4}
are sensitive to flavor hydrodynamization dynamics, thus complementing our photon study in Ref.
Kumar and Vujanovic 2025
. The present calculation accounts for heavy-quark mass scales, full phase factors, and fermion-to-boson conversion processes. Monte Carlo simulations of jet-medium interactions involving highly virtual partons, including Bayesian analyses constraining
\hat{q}

Ehlers et al. 2025
, have an unexplored theoretical systematic uncertainty due to the incomplete accounting of the Glauber-quark contribution to parton energy loss. This knowledge gap is addressed in the subsections below, from a theoretical perspective.

IV.1
Single-scattering induced emission kernel: one gluon and one quark final state

For kernel-1, a total of 23 diagrams were identified [Fig.
4
], including the left-cut and right-cut diagrams. These are presented in Appendix
B
. In order to add these diagrams, we institute
\Delta x^{-}=x^{-}_{2}-x^{-}_{1}
,
\Delta z^{-}=z^{-}_{3}-z^{-}_{2}
, and
\zeta^{-}=x^{-}_{1}-z^{-}_{2}=x^{-}_{2}-z^{-}_{3}
. The phase factors (i.e. complex exponentials) that depend on
\Delta x^{-}
are absorbed in the definition of the nucleon parton distribution function, whereas those that depend on the relative distance
\Delta z^{-}=z^{-}_{3}-z^{-}_{2}
are absorbed in the definition of the gluon/quark distribution in the medium. Following these definitions, the diagrams within each kernel are summed.

Diagrams in Fig.
4
are a common setup
Guo and Wang 2000
;
Aurenche et al. 2008a
;
Abir and Majumder 2016
;
Sirimanna et al. 2022
for quark energy loss in the nuclear medium, using the higher-twist (HT) formalism. The first HT calculation
Guo and Wang 2000
, considered a subset of diagrams in Fig.
4
for a light quark propagating through the nuclear medium. The setup in Ref.
Guo and Wang 2000
was later extended to include corrections from the heavy-quark mass scale
Abir and Majumder 2016
. Finally, Ref.
Sirimanna et al. 2022
takes into account all diagrams in Fig.
4
to explain how a light quark propagates through the nuclear medium, while our result extends those of in Ref.
Sirimanna et al. 2022
to include heavy-quark mass scales. The hadronic tensor for an incoming quark
Q
of mass
M
and flavor
f
reads:

W^{\mu\nu}_{1,\rm full}=\sum_{f}2\left[-g^{\mu\nu}_{\perp\perp}\right]e^{2}_{f}\int d(\Delta x^{-})e^{i\Delta x^{-}\left(q^{+}-\frac{M^{2}}{2q^{-}}\right)}\left\langle P\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}(\Delta x^{-})\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}(0)\right|P\right\rangle\times\mathcal{K}^{\left(Q;Q,g\right)}_{1}

(38)

where
e_{f}=2/3
for up-type quarks (
u,c,t
) and
e_{f}=-1/3
for down-type quarks (
d,s,b
). The effective medium-modified scattering kernel for type-1 processes
\mathcal{K}^{\left(Q;Q,g\right)}_{1}
is

\begin{split}\mathcal{K}^{\left(Q;Q,g\right)}_{1}&=g^{4}_{s}\int d(\Delta z^{-})d^{2}\Delta z_{\perp}\frac{dy}{2\pi}\frac{d^{2}\ell_{2\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{d^{2}k_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i\left(\Delta z^{-}\right)\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{M}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}}\\
&\times\int d\zeta^{-}\theta\left(\zeta^{-}\right)\mathcal{S}^{(Q;Q,g)}_{1}\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|{\rm Tr}\left[A^{+}\left(\zeta^{-},\Delta z^{-},\Delta z_{\perp}\right)A^{+}\left(\zeta^{-},0\right)\right]\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle,\end{split}

(39)

with

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}^{\left(Q;Q,g\right)}_{1}&=C_{A}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\times\\
&\quad\quad\quad\left\{\frac{\left(1-\frac{\eta}{2}\right)^{2}}{\left(1-\eta\right)^{2}}\frac{\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)^{2}+\kappa y^{4}M^{2}\right]\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)^{2}+y^{2}\left(1-\eta\right)^{2}M^{2}\right]^{2}}+\frac{n\left(1+\frac{\eta}{2}\right)^{2}}{\left(1+\eta\right)}\frac{\left[\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}+\kappa y^{4}M^{2}\right]}{\left[\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}+y^{2}M^{2}\right]^{2}}\left[\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{\left(\ell_{2}\right)}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}-1\right]\right\}\\
&+C_{F}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\frac{\left[\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}+\kappa y^{4}M^{2}\right]}{\left[\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}+y^{2}M^{2}\right]^{2}}\left[1-\cos\left\{G^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right](2-n)\\
&+C_{F}\left[\frac{\left(1+\eta y\right)^{2}+\left(1-y+\eta y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\left[\frac{\left\{\left(1+\eta y\right)\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-y\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right\}^{2}+\kappa y^{4}M^{2}}{J^{2}_{1}}\right]\\
&+\left[\frac{C_{A}}{2}-C_{F}\right]\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}+\eta y\left(2-y\right)}{y}\right]\left[\frac{\left(1+\eta y\right)\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}-y\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}\cdot\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}+\kappa y^{4}M^{2}}{\left[\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}+y^{2}M^{2}\right]J_{1}}\right]\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]\\
&-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\left[\frac{1-\frac{\eta}{2}}{1-\eta}\right]\left[\frac{\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)\cdot\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-y\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)+\kappa y^{4}M^{2}}{\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)^{2}+y^{2}\left(1-\eta\right)^{2}M^{2}\right]J_{1}}\right]\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]\\
&-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\left[\frac{\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)\cdot\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}+\kappa y^{4}M^{2}}{\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)^{2}+y^{2}\left(1-\eta\right)^{2}M^{2}\right]\left[\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}+y^{2}M^{2}\right]}\right]\left[1-\frac{\eta}{2}\right]\times\\
&\quad\quad\,\,\,\left\{\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}+2\cos\left\{\Delta\mathcal{G}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{1-\eta}+n\left[4\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}-2\cos\left\{\Delta\mathcal{G}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]\right\}\\
&-C_{F}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\frac{n\left[\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}+\kappa y^{4}M^{2}\right]}{\left[\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}+y^{2}M^{2}\right]^{2}}\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\end{split}

(40)

where
\Delta\mathcal{G}_{M}=\left(\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}-\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\right)
. Equation (
40
) is now examined in detail. The first two lines stem from Fig.
4
(a) combining all three cuts.

The first term on the second line is from the central cut [Eq. (

)] of Fig.

(a), while adding the corresponding left and right cuts [Eq. (

)] gives the second term of the second line.
The third line originates from Fig.

is associated with its left- and right-cut [Eq. (

The fourth line comes from the central cut of Fig.

Finally, the last line stems from adding Fig.
