)].

In Eq. (
40
), the factor
n
is included for non-central cut diagrams. Since the non-central cut diagrams give rise to two gluons on the same side of the cut-line, it imposes an additional phase-space constraint as
\theta(\Delta z^{+})
due to the time ordering of the two gluons originating from the plasma. When evaluating the hadronic tensor, the effective integral over
d(\Delta z^{+})
becomes

\displaystyle\int d(\Delta z^{+})\delta(\Delta z^{+})\theta(\Delta z^{+})=\theta\left(\Delta z^{+}=0\right)=n.

(41)

The definition of the Heaviside function at zero, encapsulated in
n
, depends on convention.
12
12

12

According to Ref.
Weinberger 1995
,
n=1
, Ref.
Bender and Orszag 2013
uses
n=1/2
, while other references, such as Ref.
Strauss 1992
, leave
n
unspecified.
A detailed discussion about the origin of
n
can be found in Appendix A of Ref.
Sirimanna et al. 2022
, which puts the bound as
0\leq n<1/2
.

IV.2
Single-scattering induced emission kernel: Two gluons in the final state

Figure 6
:
Diagrams for scattering kernel-2.

Interactions between a highly virtual incoming quark and the Glauber quark in the nuclear medium have not received the same amount of attention as those involving in-medium Glauber gluons
Idilbi and Majumder 2009
discussed above. Thus, section
IV.2
, as well as subsections
IV.3
and
IV.4
present a detailed account of interactions between a highly-virtual incoming quark and in-medium Glauber quarks. In kernel-2, considering first the channel with virtual quark and Glauber antiquark annihilation into two gluons, labeled as
q\to g+g
are shown in Fig.
6
. A complete calculation of these diagrams is presented in Appendix
C
. Adding the hadronic tensors of all diagrams (Fig.
6
) gives the following form of the hadronic tensor:

\begin{split}W^{\mu\nu}_{2,\rm full}=\sum_{f}2[-g^{\mu\nu}_{\perp\perp}]e^{2}_{f}\int d(\Delta x^{-})e^{iq^{+}(\Delta x^{-})}\left\langle P\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}(\Delta x^{-})\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}(0)\right|P\right\rangle\times\mathcal{K}^{\left(q;g,g\right)}_{2},\end{split}

(42)

where
e_{f}=2/3
for an up quark and
e_{f}=-1/3
for down and strange quarks. The effective medium-modified scattering kernel for type-2 processes
\mathcal{K}^{\left(q;g,g\right)}_{2}
is given as

\begin{split}\mathcal{K}^{\left(q;g,g\right)}_{2}&=g^{4}_{s}\int d(\Delta z^{-})d^{2}\Delta z_{\perp}\frac{dy}{2\pi}\frac{d^{2}\ell_{2\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{d^{2}k_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i\left(\Delta z^{-}\right)\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{0}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}}\\
&\times\int d\zeta^{-}\theta\left(\zeta^{-}\right)\mathcal{S}^{(q;g,g)}_{2}\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}(\zeta^{-},0)\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}(\zeta^{-},\Delta z^{-},\Delta z_{\perp})\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle,\end{split}

(43)

while

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{S}^{(q;g,g)}_{2}&=C_{A}C^{2}_{F}\left\{\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{q^{-}\left(1-y+\eta y\right)\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}+\left[\frac{1+y^{2}+\eta y^{2}\left(\eta-2\right)}{1-y+\eta y}\right]\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{yq^{-}\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)^{2}}\right\}\\
&+2C^{2}_{A}C_{F}\left[\frac{y\left(1+\eta y\right)^{2}}{1-y+\eta y}+\frac{(1+2\eta y)(1-y+\eta y)}{y}+y\left(1+\eta^{2}\right)\left(1-y+\eta y\right)\right]\frac{1}{\left(1+\eta y\right)^{2}q^{-}\left[\left(1+\eta y\right)\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-y\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right]^{2}}\\
&-\frac{C^{2}_{A}C_{F}}{2}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y+\eta y\right)^{3}}{y\left(1-y+\eta y\right)}\right]\left[\frac{\left(1+\eta y\right)\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}-y\,\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}\cdot\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}\left[\left(1+\eta y\right)\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-y\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right]^{2}}\right]\frac{1}{\left(1+\eta y\right)q^{-}}\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]\\
&-\frac{C^{2}_{A}C_{F}}{2}\left[\frac{1+y^{3}}{y\left(1-y+\eta y\right)}\right]\frac{\left[\left(1+\eta y\right)\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-y\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right]\cdot\left[\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right]}{\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)^{2}\left[\left(1+\eta y\right)\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-y\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right]^{2}}\frac{1}{\left(1+\eta y\right)q^{-}}\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]\\
&-\frac{\left(\frac{C_{A}}{2}-C_{F}\right)C_{A}C_{F}}{q^{-}\left(1-y+\eta y\right)}\left[\frac{1-y+2\eta y}{y}\right]\frac{\left[-\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}+\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}\cdot\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right]}{\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)^{2}\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}+2\cos\left\{\Delta\mathcal{G}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right],\end{split}

(44)

where
\Delta\mathcal{G}_{0}=\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{0}-\mathcal{G}^{\left(\ell_{2}\right)}_{0}
. In Eq. (
44
), the first line corresponds to the process in Fig.
6
(a) [Eq. (
212
) and (
213
)], the second line stems from the process Fig.
6
(b) [Eq. (
218
)], the third line and fourth lines are associated with interference processes shown in Fig.
6
(c,d) [Eq. (
229
) and (
230
)] where the momenta
\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}
and
\boldsymbol{p}_{2\perp}
of the two identical final state gluons are interchanged, while the fifth line corresponds to the process in Fig.
6
(e) [Eq. (
237
)].

Note, each term in Eq. (
44
) carries a suppression factor of
1/q^{-}
compared to the terms in the scattering kernel-1 [Eq. (
40
)]. This indicates that the medium-induced quark-to-gluon conversion processes of kernel-2
are suppressed by the incoming energy of the quark
q^{-}
.

IV.3
Single-scattering induced emission kernel: One quark and one antiquark in the final state

Figure 7
:
Diagrams for scattering kernel-3.

The single-scattering induced emission diagrams contributing to kernel-3 are shown in Fig.
7
. The diagrams involve a Glauber quark exchange with the medium and consist of a quark and antiquark final state. A detailed calculation of these diagrams is presented in Appendix
D
. Based on the quark flavor of the final state, the diagrams can be divided into two categories: (i)
q\to q+\bar{q}^{\prime}
and (ii)
q\to q^{\prime}+\bar{q}^{\prime}
, where
q^{\prime}
may be different from
q
. We first consider the situation of a heavy quark
(Q)
transition
Q\to Q+\bar{q}^{\prime}
in Fig.
7
(a), which gives the hadronic tensor [Eq. (
242
)]:

W^{\mu\nu}_{3,\rm full}=2[-g^{\mu\nu}_{\perp\perp}]e^{2}_{f}\int d(\Delta x^{-})e^{iq^{+}(\Delta x^{-})}e^{-i[M^{2}/(2q^{-})](\Delta x^{-})}\left\langle P\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}(\Delta x^{-})\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}(0)\right|P\right\rangle\times\mathcal{K}^{\left(Q;Q,\bar{q}^{\prime}\right)}_{3},

(45)

where
e_{f}=2/3
for a charm or top quark and
e_{f}=-1/3
for a bottom quark.
\mathcal{K}^{\left(Q;Q,\bar{q}^{\prime}\right)}_{3}
is the effective medium-modified scattering kernel for the type-3 process [Fig.
7
(a)], defined as

\begin{split}\mathcal{K}^{\left(Q;Q,\bar{q}^{\prime}\right)}_{3}&=g^{4}_{s}\sum_{f^{\prime}\in\{\bar{u},\bar{d},\bar{s}\}}\int d(\Delta z^{-})d^{2}\Delta z_{\perp}\frac{dy}{2\pi}\frac{d^{2}\ell_{2\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{d^{2}k_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i(\Delta z^{-})\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{M}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}}\\
&\times\int d\zeta^{-}\theta\left(\zeta^{-}\right)\mathcal{S}^{\left(Q;Q,\bar{q}^{\prime}\right)}_{3}\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f^{\prime}}}(\zeta^{-},0)\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f^{\prime}}}(\zeta^{-},\Delta z^{-},\Delta z_{\perp})\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle,\end{split}

(46)

with

\mathcal{S}^{\left(Q;Q,\bar{q}^{\prime}\right)}_{3}=\left[\frac{C_{F}C_{A}}{2}\right]\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\frac{1}{yq^{-}}\frac{\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)^{2}+\kappa y^{4}M^{2}\right]}{\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)^{2}+y^{2}\left(1-\eta\right)^{2}M^{2}\right]^{2}}\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right],

(47)

where
M
is the mass of the quark with flavor
f
. Note that Eq. (
45
) follows from Eq. (
242
). The second option in Fig.
7
(a) is one in which the light quark
q
has a different flavor than the light antiquark
\bar{q}^{\prime}
, and in that case one simply sets
M=0
in Eq. (
46
) and Eq. (
47
).

The other process contributing to kernel-3 is
q\to q^{\prime}+\bar{q}^{\prime}
in Fig.
7
(b), where the quark
q^{\prime}
, spanning both light and heavy varieties, has a different flavor from the light quark
q
. For that case, a fixed light quark
q
with flavor
f
gives the hadronic tensor [Eq. (
247
)]:

\begin{split}W^{\mu\nu}_{3,\rm final}&=2\left[-g^{\mu\nu}_{\perp\perp}\right]e^{2}_{f}\int d(\Delta x^{-})e^{iq^{+}(\Delta x^{-})}\left\langle P\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}(\Delta x^{-})\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{f}(0)\right|P\right\rangle\times\mathcal{K}^{\left(q;q^{\prime},\bar{q}^{\prime}\right)}_{3},\end{split}

(48)

with

\begin{split}\mathcal{K}^{\left(q;q^{\prime},\bar{q}^{\prime}\right)}_{3}&=g^{4}_{s}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}f^{\prime}\neq f\\
f^{\prime}\in\{u,d,s,c,b,t\}\end{subarray}}\int d(\Delta z^{-})d^{2}\Delta z_{\perp}\frac{dy}{2\pi}\frac{d^{2}\ell_{2\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{d^{2}k_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{1}(\Delta z^{-})}e^{-i\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}}\\
&\times\int d\zeta^{-}\theta(\zeta^{-})\mathcal{S}^{\left(q;q^{\prime},\bar{q}^{\prime}\right)}_{3}\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}(\zeta^{-},0)\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}(\zeta^{-},\Delta z^{-},\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp})\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle,\end{split}

(49)

where,
\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{1}
is defined in Eq. (
135
) and is dependent on
f^{\prime}
, while

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}^{\left(q;q^{\prime},\bar{q}^{\prime}\right)}_{3}=\left[\frac{C_{F}C_{A}}{2}\right]\frac{1}{q^{-}}\frac{\left[y^{2}+\left(1-y+\eta y\right)^{2}\right]}{\left(1+\eta y\right)^{2}\left[\left\{\left(1+\eta y\right)\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-y\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right\}^{2}+M^{2}_{f^{\prime}}\left(1+\eta y\right)^{2}\right]}.\end{split}

(50)

The last contribution to kernel-3 comes from the process
q\to q+\bar{q}
, where the incoming and outgoing quarks are of the same light flavor. In that scenario, all diagrams in Fig.
7
contribute, i.e. Eq. (
242
), Eq. (
247
), and Eq. (
256
), giving

\begin{split}W^{\mu\nu}_{3,\rm final}&=2\left[-g^{\mu\nu}_{\perp\perp}\right]e^{2}_{f}\int d(\Delta x^{-})e^{iq^{+}(\Delta x^{-})}\left\langle P\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}(\Delta x^{-})\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{f}(0)\right|P\right\rangle\times\mathcal{K}^{\left(q;q,\bar{q}\right)}_{3},\end{split}

(51)

with

\begin{split}\mathcal{K}^{\left(q;q,\bar{q}\right)}_{3}&=g^{4}_{s}\int d(\Delta z^{-})d^{2}\Delta z_{\perp}\frac{dy}{2\pi}\frac{d^{2}\ell_{2\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{d^{2}k_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i(\Delta z^{-})\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{0}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}}\\
&\times\int d\zeta^{-}\theta\left(\zeta^{-}\right)\mathcal{S}^{\left(q;q,\bar{q}\right)}_{3}\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}(\zeta^{-},0)\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}(\zeta^{-},\Delta z^{-},\Delta z_{\perp})\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle,\end{split}

(52)

and

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathcal{S}^{\left(q;q,\bar{q}\right)}_{3}&=\left[\frac{C_{F}C_{A}}{2}\right]\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\frac{1}{yq^{-}}\frac{1}{\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)^{2}}\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]\\
&+\left[\frac{C_{F}C_{A}}{2}\right]\frac{1}{q^{-}}\frac{\left[y^{2}+\left(1-y+\eta y\right)^{2}\right]}{\left(1+\eta y\right)^{2}\left[\left\{\left(1+\eta y\right)\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-y\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right\}^{2}+\left(1+\eta y\right)^{2}\right]}\\
&-\left[C_{F}C_{A}\left(C_{F}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\right)\right]\frac{1}{yq^{-}}\left[\frac{1-y+\eta y}{(1+\eta y)(1-\eta)}\right]\frac{J_{2}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)^{2}\left[\left(1+\eta y\right)\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-y\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right]^{2}}\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]\end{split}

(53)

where
J_{2}
and
\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{0}
are defined in Appendix
A
. The expression of the scattering kernel [Eq. (
47
), Eq. (
49
), and Eq. (
53
)] contains a factor of
1/q^{-}
, therefore, like kernel-2, the processes in kernel-3 are suppressed by a factor of the incoming energy of the quark
q^{-}
.

IV.4
Single-scattering induced emission kernel: Two quarks in the final state

Figure 8
:
Diagrams for scattering kernel-4.

The single-scattering induced emission diagrams with two quarks in the final state are presented in Fig.
8
. The evaluation of these diagrams is discussed in Appendix
E
.
The case with two quarks in the final state is further subdivided into three categories: (i) a final state with a heavy quark
Q
and a light quark of flavor
q^{\prime}
, (ii) a final state with a light quark
q
and another light quark of a different flavor
q^{\prime}
, and (iii) final state with two light quarks of the same flavor
q
. The Feynmann diagram in Fig.
8
(b) contributes solely to final state of type (iii). For a given heavy quark
Q
participating in the process
Q\to Q+q^{\prime}
, the hadronic tensor is [Eq. (
261
)]:

\begin{split}W^{\mu\nu}_{4,\rm full}&=2[-g^{\mu\nu}_{\perp\perp}]e^{2}_{f}\int d(\Delta x^{-})e^{iq^{+}(\Delta x^{-})}e^{-i[M^{2}/(2q^{-})](\Delta x^{-})}\left\langle P\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}(\Delta x^{-})\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}(0)\right|P\right\rangle\times\mathcal{K}^{\left(Q;Q,q^{\prime}\right)}_{4},\end{split}

(54)

where
e_{f}=2/3
for a charm or top quark and
e_{f}=-1/3
for a bottom quark.
\mathcal{K}^{\left(Q;Q,q^{\prime}\right)}_{4}
is the effective medium-modified scattering kernel for the type-4 process [Fig.
8
(a)], defined as

\begin{split}\mathcal{K}^{\left(Q;Q,q^{\prime}\right)}_{4}&=g^{4}_{s}\sum_{f^{\prime}\in\{u,d,s\}}\int d(\Delta z^{-})d^{2}\Delta z_{\perp}\frac{dy}{2\pi}\frac{d^{2}\ell_{2\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{d^{2}k_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i(\Delta z^{-})\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{M}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}}\\
&\times\int d\zeta^{-}\theta\left(\zeta^{-}\right)\mathcal{S}^{\left(Q;Q,q^{\prime}\right)}_{4}\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f^{\prime}}}(\zeta^{-},\Delta z^{-},\Delta z_{\perp})\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f^{\prime}}}(\zeta^{-},0)\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle,\end{split}

(55)

where
f^{\prime}
spans the three light flavors, while

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}^{\left(Q;Q,q^{\prime}\right)}_{4}&=\left[\frac{C_{F}C_{A}}{2}\right]\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\frac{1}{yq^{-}}\frac{\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)^{2}+\kappa y^{4}M^{2}\right]}{\left[\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)^{2}+y^{2}\left(1-\eta\right)^{2}M^{2}\right]^{2}}\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right].\end{split}

(56)

For an incoming light-flavor quark
q
participating in the process
q\to q+q^{\prime}
, with
q^{\prime}
being of different flavor from
q
, one simply sets
M=0
in Eq. (
55
) and Eq. (
56
).

Finally, both diagrams in Fig.
8
contribute to the process
q\to q+q
, where all light flavors
q
are the same. The hadronic tensor and scattering kernel, combining Eq. (
261
), Eq. (
262
), and Eq. (
271
), gives:

\displaystyle W^{\mu\nu}_{4,c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2[-g^{\mu\nu}_{\perp\perp}]e^{2}_{f}\int d(\Delta x^{-})e^{iq^{+}(\Delta x^{-})}\left\langle P\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}(\Delta x^{-})\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}(0)\right|P\right\rangle\times\mathcal{K}^{\left(q;q,q\right)}_{4}

(57)

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{\left(q;q,q\right)}_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{4}_{s}\int d(\Delta z^{-})d^{2}\Delta z_{\perp}\frac{dy}{2\pi}\frac{d^{2}\ell_{2\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{d^{2}k_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i(\Delta z^{-})\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{0}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}},

(58)

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\int d\zeta^{-}\theta\left(\zeta^{-}\right)\mathcal{S}^{\left(q;q,q\right)}_{4}\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}(\zeta^{-},\Delta z^{-},\Delta z_{\perp})\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}(\zeta^{-},0)\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle,

with

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{\left(q;q,q\right)}_{4}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\frac{C_{F}C_{A}}{2}\right]\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\frac{1}{yq^{-}}\frac{1}{\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right)^{2}}\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left[\frac{C_{F}C_{A}}{2}\right]\left[\frac{1+y^{2}}{1-y}\right]\frac{1}{q^{-}}\frac{1}{\left(1-y+\eta y\right)\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{C_{F}C_{A}\left(C_{F}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\right)}{(1-y)yq^{-}}\frac{\left[-\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}+\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}\cdot\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\right]}{\left(1-\eta\right)\left(\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}-\boldsymbol{k}_{2\perp}\right)^{2}\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}+2\cos\left\{\Delta\mathcal{G}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right],

where
\Delta\mathcal{G}_{0}=\left(\mathcal{G}^{(p_{2})}_{0}-\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\right)
.

Like kernel-2 and kernel-3, the scattering kernel-4 contains an additional factor of
1/q^{-}
, therefore, the processes in kernel-4 are also suppressed by the incoming energy of the quark
q^{-}
. However, note that suppression by
1/q^{-}
does not imply that kernel-2 through kernel-4 should not be present inside a jet Monte Carlo simulation.
While
q^{-}
is a dynamical quantity and a large quantity for the first split, it decreases as the shower is generated with subsequent splits. Moreover, as the system created in relativistic heavy-ion collisions transitions from the early-time glasma to the QGP, quark occupation number in the medium increases, and hence, the jet energy loss starts to become sensitive to kernel-2 through kernel-4.

The transport coefficient
\hat{q}
has been estimated for a quark that traverses QGP and cold nuclear matter. The phenomenological studies carried out by JET
Burke et al. 2014
and JETSCAPE
Ehlers et al. 2025
Collaborations indicate that
\hat{q}
is
1-2
GeV
2
/fm at temperatures
T\in[300,400]
MeV in RHIC and LHC collisions.
There also exist estimates of
\hat{q}
based on jet quenching in DIS for cold nuclei. Using an ideal quark gas equation of state with three valence quarks at normal nucleon density in a large nucleus,
\hat{q}
has been estimated to be
0.02-0.06
GeV
2
/fm
Deng and Wang 2010
;
Burke et al. 2014
. A more recent estimate
Ru et al. 2021
based on the global fit to experimental data for semi-inclusive e-A DIS, Drell-Yan (DY) dilepton, and heavy quarkonium production in proton-nucleus (p-A) collisions also yields a similar value of
\hat{q}
in cold nuclear matter. These estimates indicate that the transport coefficient
\hat{q}
is an order of magnitude higher in hot QGP compared to the large nucleus. Assuming a similar behavior is true for the jet transport coefficient involving the fermionic correlator (see next section), one could assert that the new contributions presented in kernel-2 through kernel-4 would play a more significant role for jets traversing through QGP compared to jets in cold nuclear matter, such as in DIS.

V
Collinear expansion and jet transport coefficients at next-to-leading order (NLO) and next-to-leading twist (NLT)

Having presented the full scattering kernel for a hard quark traversing the nuclear medium, a Taylor expansion of four scattering kernels
\mathcal{S}_{i}
around
\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}=\boldsymbol{0}_{\perp}
and
k^{-}=0
is now presented, as has been done in prior higher-twist calculations
Abir and Majumder 2016
;
Sirimanna et al. 2022
. In general, the expansion takes the form

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{(a;b,c)}_{i}\left(\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp},k^{-}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}^{(a;b,c)}_{i}\left(\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}=\boldsymbol{0}_{\perp},k^{-}=0\right)+\left.\frac{\partial\mathcal{S}^{(a;b,c)}_{i}}{\partial k^{\rho}_{\perp}}\right|_{k=0}k^{\rho}_{\perp}+\frac{1}{2!}\left.\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{S}^{(a;b,c)}_{i}}{\partial k^{\rho}_{\perp}\partial k^{\sigma}_{\perp}}\right|_{k=0}k^{\rho}_{\perp}\otimes k^{\sigma}_{\perp}+\cdots

(60)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\left.\frac{\partial\mathcal{S}^{(a;b,c)}_{i}}{\partial k^{-}}\right|_{k=0}k^{-}+\frac{1}{2!}\left.\frac{\partial^{2}\mathcal{S}^{(a;b,c)}_{i}}{\partial{k^{-}}^{2}}\right|_{k=0}k^{-}\otimes k^{-}+\cdots,

where
|_{k=0}
is shorthand notation for all components of
k
being evaluated to zero, and
i
spans the four kinds of kernels discussed herein, and
(a;b,c)
represents the species involved in the
1\to 2
process. For example, for a heavy quark radiating a gluon,
Q\to Q+g
is labeled
(a;b,c)=(Q;Q,g)
. Assuming a homogeneous and isotropic nuclear medium leads to a vanishing second term in Eq. (
60
), after integration over
k_{\perp}
is performed. Homogeneity and isotropy further entails that the third term in Eq. (
60
) is non-zero only when
\rho=\sigma
.

Applying collinear expansion, the effective medium-modified scattering kernel
\mathcal{K}^{(a;b,c)}_{i}
can be written as

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}^{(a;b,c)}_{i}=g^{2}_{s}\int\frac{dy}{2\pi}\frac{d^{2}\ell_{2\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}d\zeta^{-}\left[\mathcal{R}^{(a;b,c)}_{(i;0)}\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{(i;0)}+\left(\frac{1}{2!}\mathcal{R}^{(a;b,c)}_{(i;T,2)}\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{(i;T,2)}+\frac{1}{4!}\mathcal{R}^{(a;b,c)}_{(i;T,4)}\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{(i;T,4)}+\cdots\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\left(\mathcal{R}^{(a;b,c)}_{(i;L,1)}\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{(i;L,1)}+\frac{1}{2!}\mathcal{R}^{(a;b,c)}_{(i;L,2)}\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{(i;L,2)}+\cdots\right)\right],

(61)

where
\mathcal{R}^{(a;b,c)}_{(i;0)}
is the zeroth order term in the Taylor expansion of the
i
-th collisional kernel, with
\mathcal{R}^{(a;b,c)}_{(i;L,j)}
representing the
j^{\rm th}
order derivative of
\mathcal{S}^{(a;b,c)}_{i}
along the
k^{-}
–direction, and
\mathcal{R}^{(a;b,c)}_{(i;T,j)}
denotes the
j^{\rm th}
order derivative of
\mathcal{S}^{(a;b,c)}_{i}
along
k_{\perp}
–direction. Note that
\mathcal{R}
’s depend solely on the momentum fraction
y
,
\zeta^{-}
, and
\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}
.

The operator expectation values
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{(i;0)}
,
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{(i;T,j)}
, and
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{(i;L,i)}
represent two-point gluonic or fermionic jet-medium correlations (also called jet-medium transport coefficients). The factors of
k^{\rho}_{\perp}\otimes k^{\sigma}_{\perp}
and
k^{-}\otimes k^{-}
in the Taylor series expansion are converted into spacetime derivatives acting on the gluonic fields
A^{\mu}
or fermionic quark fields
\psi
(see below) and are thereby absorbed in the definition of jet-medium transport coefficients. The expectation value
\hat{\mathcal{O}}
of the two-point gluonic correlator will be labeled
\hat{\mathcal{A}}
, while
\hat{\mathcal{F}}
represents the expectation value of the two-point fermionic correlator. The superscripts
{(a;b,c)}
are only really needed for the envelopes
\mathcal{R}^{(a;b,c)}_{(i)}
to identify the specific processes within a kernel.

The in-medium, stochastic, gluon-induced momentum broadening in the transverse direction of a quark jet is encapsulated in the operator
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{(1;T,2)}
, labeled as
\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{(T,2)}
from now on, and more commonly known as the jet-medium transport coefficient
\hat{q}

Majumder and Muller 2008
. Similarly,
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{(1;L,1)}
, from now on labeled as
\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{(L,1)}
, is known as
\hat{e}
, quantified the longitudinal momentum drag imparted by the nuclear medium on the jet parton.
13
13

13

The longitudinal momentum broadening
\hat{e}_{2}
would be given by
\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{(1;L,2)}=\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{(L,2)}
, which the reader can straightforwardly derive using the process shown here.
There exists lattice determinations of the jet transport coefficient
\hat{q}
containing the gluonic correlator
\hat{\mathcal{A}}

Kumar et al. 2022
;
Weber et al. 2023
;
Panero et al. 2014
;
Majumder 2013b
;
Kumar et al. 2021
;
Kumar et al. 2019
;
Kumar et al. 2018
, however, the estimates of jet transport coefficient containing fermionic correlator
\hat{\mathcal{F}}
is still unknown.

Only kernel-1 involves the gluonic two-point correlator, which are given by:

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{(1;0)}=\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{0}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{2}_{s}\int d(\Delta z^{-})d^{2}\Delta z_{\perp}\frac{d^{2}k_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i\Delta z^{-}\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{M}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}}

(62)

\displaystyle\times\theta(\zeta^{-})\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|{\rm Tr}\left[A^{+}(\zeta^{-},\Delta z^{-},\Delta z_{\perp})A^{+}(\zeta^{-},0)\right]\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{(1;L,1)}=\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{(L,1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{2}_{s}\int d(\Delta z^{-})d^{2}\Delta z_{\perp}\frac{d^{2}k_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i\Delta z^{-}\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{M}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}}

(63)

\displaystyle\times\theta(\zeta^{-})\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|{\rm Tr}\left[i\left\{\partial^{-}A^{+}(\zeta^{-},\Delta z^{-},\Delta z_{\perp})\right\}A^{+}(\zeta^{-},0)\right]\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{(1;T,2)}=\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{(T,2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{2}_{s}\int d(\Delta z^{-})d^{2}\Delta z_{\perp}\frac{d^{2}k_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i\Delta z^{-}\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{M}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}}

(64)

\displaystyle\times\theta(\zeta^{-})\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|{\rm Tr}\left[\left\{\partial_{\perp}A^{+}(\zeta^{-},\Delta z^{-},\Delta z_{\perp})\right\}\left\{\partial_{\perp}A^{+}(\zeta^{-},0)\right\}\right]\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle.

Note that
\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{(L,1)}
and
\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{T,2}
depend explicitly on
\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}
via the function
\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{M}
, thus these are transverse-momentum-dependent gluon parton distribution functions (TMD-gPDFs)
Kumar et al. 2025
. Furthermore,
\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{0}
gives rise to a gauge correction to the nuclear PDF in the limit
\boldsymbol{\ell}_{\perp}\rightarrow\boldsymbol{0}_{\perp}
and
\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\rightarrow\boldsymbol{0}_{\perp}

Wang and Guo 2001
, and is therefore not a jet-medium transport coefficient.

On the other hand, kernel-2 through kernel-4 all include the fermionic two-point correlators. For kernel-2 and kernel-3, the two-point functions are

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{(i;0)}=\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{(0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{2}_{s}\int d(\Delta z^{-})d^{2}\Delta z_{\perp}\frac{d^{2}k_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i\Delta z^{-}\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{M}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}}

(65)

\displaystyle\times\theta(\zeta^{-})\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}\left(\zeta^{-},0\right)\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}\left(\zeta^{-},\Delta z^{-},\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}\right)\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{(i;L,1)}=\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{(L,1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{2}_{s}\int d(\Delta z^{-})d^{2}\Delta z_{\perp}\frac{d^{2}k_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i\Delta z^{-}\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{M}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}}

(66)

\displaystyle\times\theta(\zeta^{-})\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|i\left\{\partial^{-}\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}\left(\zeta^{-},0\right)\right\}\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}\left(\zeta^{-},\Delta z^{-},\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}\right)\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{(i;T,2)}=\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{(T,2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{2}_{s}\int d(\Delta z^{-})d^{2}\Delta z_{\perp}\frac{d^{2}k_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i\Delta z^{-}\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{M}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}}

(67)

\displaystyle\times\theta(\zeta^{-})\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|\left\{\partial_{\perp}\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}\left(\zeta^{-},0\right)\right\}\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\left\{\partial_{\perp}\psi_{{}_{f}}\left(\zeta^{-},\Delta z^{-},\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}\right)\right\}\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle,

where the index
i\in\{2,3\}
was suppressed in
\hat{\mathcal{F}}
, given that kernel-2 and kernel-3 involve identical jet-medium transport coefficients.
The
\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{(0)},\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{(L,1)}
, and
\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{(T,2)}
distribution functions depend explicitly on
\boldsymbol{\ell}_{2\perp}
via the function
\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{M}
, where for light flavors
\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{M}\to\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{0}
, and are transverse-momentum-dependent quark parton distribution functions (TMD-qPDFs). Note that the zeroth-order term in Eq. (
65
) does not represent a gauge term and cannot be absorbed in the nuclear PDF; instead, it gives rise to the leading term contributing to the parton energy loss.

For kernel-4, the fermionic two-point function is:

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{(4;0)}=\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{(4;0)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{2}_{s}\int d(\Delta z^{-})d^{2}\Delta z_{\perp}\frac{d^{2}k_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i\Delta z^{-}\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{M}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}}

(68)

\displaystyle\times\theta(\zeta^{-})\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}(\zeta^{-},\Delta z^{-},\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp})\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}\left(\zeta^{-},0\right)\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{(4;L,1)}=\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{(4;L,1)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{2}_{s}\int d(\Delta z^{-})d^{2}\Delta z_{\perp}\frac{d^{2}k_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i\Delta z^{-}\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{M}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}}

(69)

\displaystyle\times\theta(\zeta^{-})\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|i\left\{\partial^{-}\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}(\zeta^{-},\Delta z^{-},\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp})\right\}\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\psi_{{}_{f}}\left(\zeta^{-},0\right)\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle,

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{(4;T,2)}=\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{(4;T,2)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle g^{2}_{s}\int d(\Delta z^{-})d^{2}\Delta z_{\perp}\frac{d^{2}k_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i\Delta z^{-}\mathcal{H}^{(\ell_{2},p_{2})}_{M}}e^{i\boldsymbol{k}_{\perp}\cdot\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp}}

(70)

\displaystyle\times\theta(\zeta^{-})\left\langle P_{A-1}\left|\left\{\partial_{\perp}\bar{\psi}_{{}_{f}}(\zeta^{-},\Delta z^{-},\Delta\boldsymbol{z}_{\perp})\right\}\frac{\gamma^{+}}{4}\left\{\partial_{\perp}\psi_{{}_{f}}\left(\zeta^{-},0\right)\right\}\right|P_{A-1}\right\rangle,

where the arguments of
\psi
and
\bar{\psi}
are reversed compared to the second and third kernels, thus an additional subscript is present.

V.1
Expansion of the one quark and gluon final state

For the case where a heavy quark
Q
undergoes
Q\to Q+g
illustrated in Fig.
4

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}^{\left(Q;Q,g\right)}_{\left(1;T,2\right)}&=C_{A}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\mathfrak{a}+C_{F}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\mathfrak{b}+C_{F}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\mathfrak{c}\\
&+\left[\frac{C_{A}}{2}-C_{F}\right]\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\mathfrak{d}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\mathfrak{e}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\mathfrak{f}\\
&-C_{F}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\mathfrak{g},\end{split}

(71)

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4}{\left(1+\chi\right)^{2}}\left[\left[1-2\left(\frac{3+\kappa y^{2}\chi}{1+\chi}\right)+6\frac{\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)}{\left(1+\chi\right)^{2}}\right]\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}}\right.

(72)

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+2\beta\left[3+\kappa y^{2}\chi-4\left(\frac{1+\kappa y^{2}\chi}{1+\chi}\right)\right]\frac{\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+2\beta^{2}\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right],

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

(73)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4}{\left(1+\chi\right)^{2}}\left[1-2\left(\frac{3+\kappa y^{2}\chi}{1+\chi}\right)+6\left(\frac{1+\kappa y^{2}\chi}{\left(1+\chi\right)^{2}}\right)\right]\frac{y^{2}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}},

(74)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4y^{2}}{\left(1+\chi\right)^{3}}\left[2\left(\frac{1+\kappa y^{2}\chi}{1+\chi}\right)-\left(2+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)\right]\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}},

(75)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4}{\left(1+\chi\right)^{2}}\left[\left[y-\frac{\left(1+y\right)^{2}+\left(1+y^{2}\right)\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)}{\left(1+\chi\right)}+\frac{2\left(1+y+y^{2}\right)\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)}{\left(1+\chi\right)^{2}}\right]\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}}\right.

(76)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\left.+2\beta\left[\left(2+y+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)-\frac{2\left(1+y\right)\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)}{\left(1+\chi\right)}\right]\frac{\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\left.+2\beta^{2}\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right],

\displaystyle\mathfrak{f}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4}{\left(1+\chi\right)^{2}}\left[2\left[\frac{2\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)}{\left(1+\chi\right)^{2}}-\frac{(2+\kappa y^{2}\chi)}{\left(1+\chi\right)}\right]\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}}\right.

(77)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\left.+2\beta\left[2+\kappa y^{2}\chi-\frac{2\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)}{\left(1+\chi\right)}\right]\frac{\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}+2\beta^{2}\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}-2\beta^{2}\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+n\left[\frac{2\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)}{\left(1+\chi\right)^{2}}-\frac{(2+\kappa y^{2}\chi)}{\left(1+\chi\right)}\right]\frac{\left[4\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}-2\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}}+2n\beta^{2}\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)\right],

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

(78)

and

\displaystyle\chi

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{y^{2}M^{2}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}},

(79)

\displaystyle\beta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\zeta^{-}}{2y\left(1-y\right)q^{-}}.

(80)

The expressions for
\mathfrak{a}
through
\mathfrak{g}
are directly comparable to the results in Ref.
Sirimanna et al. 2022
, after taking the massless limit
\chi\to 0
. There are three main differences between our result and that of Ref.
Sirimanna et al. 2022
. The first discrepancy occurs at the term
\mathfrak{c}
, which originates from the diagram in Fig.
13
(b) and corresponds to
H_{10}
in Ref.
Sirimanna et al. 2022
. Reference
Sirimanna et al. 2022
found that if one sets the quark mass to zero before Taylor expanding in
k_{\perp}
, the process in Fig.
13
(b) does not contribute to the medium-modified kernel-1. Our result shows instead that if one performs the Taylor expansion in
k_{\perp}
first and then takes the massless limit
\chi\to 0
the opposite conclusion is reached: the process in Fig.
13
(b) does contribute to medium modifications to kernel-1. Furthermore, the QCD Casimir coefficient associated with
\mathfrak{d}
is different in Ref.
Sirimanna et al. 2022
. Finally,
\mathfrak{f}
differs from Ref.
Sirimanna et al. 2022
even in the massless limit: Eq. (67) in Ref.
Sirimanna et al. 2022
quotes a phase factor of
\left[2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}-2\cos\left\{\Delta\mathcal{G}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]
, while we obtain
\left[4\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}-2\cos\left\{\Delta\mathcal{G}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]
in the massless limit from the left and right cut interference term. Thus, the third line in
\mathfrak{f}
differs from Eq. (68) in Ref.
Sirimanna et al. 2022
. To summarize: the first two lines of
\mathfrak{f}
are derived from central cut diagrams, while the left- and right-cut interference terms are in the last line.

The expression in Eq. (
71
) can be simplified further by combining all
C_{A}
terms together; and similarly for
C_{F}
terms. Doing so yields the following:

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}^{\left(Q;Q,g\right)}_{\left(1;T,2\right)}&=C_{A}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\mathfrak{A}+C_{F}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\mathfrak{B}\\
\end{split}

(81)

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4}{\left(1+\chi\right)^{3}}\left[\frac{\left\{2-y-\left[2+4n-(5+2n)\kappa y^{2}+2\kappa y^{3}\right]\chi+\left[2-y-(1+2n)\kappa y^{2}\right]\chi^{2}\right\}}{2\left(1+\chi\right)}\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}}\right.

(82)

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-\beta\left(2-y\right)\left[1-\left(1-2\kappa y^{2}\right)\chi\right]\frac{\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}+n\left[\frac{\chi\left(2-\kappa y^{2}+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)}{\left(1+\chi\right)}\right]\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}}+(1-n)\beta^{2}(1+\chi)(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi)\right]

\displaystyle\mathfrak{B}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4y^{2}}{\left(1+\chi\right)^{4}}\left[\left[\frac{1-4\left(1-\kappa y^{2}\right)\chi+\left(1-2\kappa y^{2}\right)\chi^{2}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}}\right]+\left[\chi\left(2-\kappa y^{2}+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)\right]\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}}\right]

(83)

In terms of the longitudinal direction,

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}^{\left(Q;Q,g\right)}_{\left(1;L,1\right)}&=C_{A}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}+C_{A}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}+C_{F}\left[\frac{1+(1-y)^{2}}{y}\right]\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}+\left[\frac{C_{A}}{2}-C_{F}\right]\left[\frac{1+(1-y)^{2}}{y}\right]\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}\\
&-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\mathfrak{e}^{\prime}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\mathfrak{f}^{\prime}-C_{F}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}\end{split}

(84)

with

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{yq^{-}\left(1+\chi\right)^{2}}\left[\left[1+\kappa y^{2}\chi+\frac{4\chi\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)}{1+\chi}\right]\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}\right.

(85)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\left.+2\beta\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)\left[\left(1+\chi\right)\left(\frac{1-2y}{1-y}\right)-2\chi\right]\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right],

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0

(86)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{yq^{-}\left(1+\chi\right)^{2}}\left[(2-y)y\kappa\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)+y\left[1-\frac{2\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)}{1+\chi}\right]\right]\frac{2}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}

(87)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{yq^{-}\left(1+\chi\right)^{2}}\left[(2-y)y\kappa\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)+y\left[1-\frac{2\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)}{1+\chi}\right]\right]\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}

(88)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{yq^{-}\left(1+\chi\right)^{2}}\left[\left[\frac{1+\kappa y^{2}\chi}{2}+\frac{2\left(\chi-y\right)\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)}{1+\chi}\right]\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}\right.

(89)

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\left.2\beta\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)\left[\left(1+\chi\right)\left(\frac{1-2y}{1-y}\right)-2\chi\right]\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\mathfrak{f}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{yq^{-}\left(1+\chi\right)^{2}}\left[\left[1+\kappa y^{2}\chi+\frac{4\chi\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)}{\left(1+\chi\right)}\right]\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}\right.

(90)

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+2\beta(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi)\left[\left(1+\chi\right)\left(\frac{1-2y}{1-y}\right)-2\chi\right]\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad-n\left[\frac{\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)}{2}-\frac{2\chi\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)}{(1+\chi)}\right]\frac{\left[4\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}-2\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}\right]

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

(91)

where
\beta
is defined in Eq. (
80
).
Note that the first two lines
\mathfrak{f}^{\prime}
stem from central cut diagrams, while the last line is from the left- and right-cut interference term. Simplifying Eq. (
84
) yields

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}^{\left(Q;Q,g\right)}_{\left(1;L,1\right)}&=C_{A}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}+C_{F}\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\mathfrak{B}^{\prime},\end{split}

(92)

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{A}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4yq^{-}\left(1+\chi\right)^{3}}\left[\left[a+b\chi+c\chi^{2}\right]\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}+2n\left(1-3\chi\right)\frac{(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi)}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}\right],

\displaystyle a

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 1-2n+\left(2+4\kappa\right)y-2\kappa y^{2},

\displaystyle b

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 5+6n+\left(2+4\kappa\right)y-\left(1+2n\right)\kappa y^{2}+2\left(2-y\right)\kappa^{2}y^{3},

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(5+6n+4\kappa y-2\kappa y^{2}\right)\kappa y^{2},

(93)

and

\begin{split}\mathfrak{B}^{\prime}&=\frac{1}{yq^{-}\left(1+\chi\right)^{2}}\left[\left(2-y\right)y\kappa\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)+y\left[1-\frac{2\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)}{1+\chi}\right]\right]\frac{\left[2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}.\end{split}

(94)

V.2
Expansion of the two-gluon final state

To extract the jet-medium transport coefficient associated with
q\to g+g
, one Taylor expands
\mathcal{S}_{2}
around
k_{\perp}=0
giving

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}^{\left(q;g,g\right)}_{\left(2;T,2\right)}&=C_{A}C^{2}_{F}\mathfrak{a}+2C^{2}_{A}C_{F}\mathfrak{b}-\frac{C^{2}_{A}C_{F}}{2}\mathfrak{c}-\frac{C^{2}_{A}C_{F}}{2}\mathfrak{d}-C_{A}C_{F}\left(\frac{C_{A}}{2}-C_{F}\right)\mathfrak{e},\end{split}

(95)

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4\left(1+y^{2}\right)}{yq^{-}\left(1-y\right)}\left[\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}}-\frac{2\beta}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}+2\beta^{2}\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right],

(96)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\frac{y}{1-y}+\frac{1-y}{y}+y\left(1-y\right)\right]\frac{4y^{2}}{q^{-}\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}},

(97)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0,

(98)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4\left(1+y^{3}\right)}{q^{-}\left(1-y\right)}\left[\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}}-\frac{2\beta}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}+\frac{2\beta^{2}}{y}\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right],

(99)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{4\beta^{2}}{yq^{-}}\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right].

(100)

Performing a Taylor expansion of
\mathcal{S}_{2}
around
k^{-}=0
yields:

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}^{\left(q;g,g\right)}_{\left(2;L,1\right)}&=C_{A}C^{2}_{F}\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}+2C^{2}_{A}C_{F}\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}-\frac{C^{2}_{A}C_{F}}{2}\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}-\frac{C^{2}_{A}C_{F}}{2}\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}+C_{A}C_{F}\left(\frac{C_{A}}{2}-C_{F}\right)\mathfrak{e}^{\prime},\end{split}

(101)

where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{a}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{3}{y\left[\left(1-y\right)q^{-}\right]^{2}\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]+\frac{2\beta\left(1+y^{2}\right)}{\left(yq^{-}\right)^{2}\left(1-y\right)}\left(\frac{1-2y}{1-y}\right)\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\},

(102)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{b}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\left(q^{-}\right)^{2}\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}\left\{y+\frac{1}{y}-\frac{y}{(1-y)^{2}}-2\left[\frac{y}{1-y}+\frac{1-y}{y}+2y\left(1-y\right)\right]\right\},

(103)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{c}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[\frac{2y\left[1+\left(1-y\right)^{3}\right]-3}{y\left(1-y\right)^{2}}\right]\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\left(q^{-}\right)^{2}\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}},

(104)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{d}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\left(1+y^{3}\right)}{y\left(1-y\right)\left(q^{-}\right)^{2}}\left[\frac{2\beta}{y}\left(\frac{1-2y}{1-y}\right)\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}-\left(\frac{3-2y}{1-y}\right)\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}\right],

(105)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{e}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{y(1-y)\left(q^{-}\right)^{2}}\left[\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}+\frac{\beta(1-2y)}{y}\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right].

(106)

V.3
Expansion of the one quark and one antiquark final state

For the case where a heavy quark
Q
undergoes
Q\to Q+\bar{q}^{\prime}
illustrated in Fig.
7
(a)

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}^{\left(Q;Q,\bar{q}^{\prime}\right)}_{\left(3;T,2\right)}&=\left[\frac{C_{F}C_{A}}{2}\right]\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\frac{1}{yq^{-}}\frac{4}{\left(1+\chi\right)^{2}}\times\\
&\times\left[\left[1-2\left(\frac{3+\kappa y^{2}\chi}{1+\chi}\right)+6\frac{\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)}{\left(1+\chi\right)^{2}}\right]\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}}\right.\\
&\left.\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+2\beta\left[3+\kappa y^{2}\chi-4\left(\frac{1+\kappa y^{2}\chi}{1+\chi}\right)\right]\frac{\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}\right.\\
&\left.\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+2\beta^{2}\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right],\end{split}

(107)

while

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}^{\left(Q;Q,\bar{q}^{\prime}\right)}_{\left(3;L,1\right)}&=\left[\frac{C_{F}C_{A}}{2}\right]\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\frac{1}{\left(yq^{-}\right)^{2}}\frac{2\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)}{\left(1+\chi\right)^{2}}\times\\
&\times\left[\frac{2\chi}{\left(1+\chi\right)}\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}+\beta\left[\left(1+\chi\right)\left(\frac{1-2y}{1-y}\right)-2\chi\right]\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right].\end{split}

(108)

For the case a light quark
q
undergoes
q\to q+\bar{q}^{\prime}
illustrated in Fig.
7
(a), one simply sets
M=0
the equations above.

In the situation where a light quark gets annihilated in the medium, as explored in
q\to q^{\prime}+\bar{q}^{\prime}
illustrated in Fig.
7
(b), one obtains

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}^{\left(q;q^{\prime},\bar{q}^{\prime}\right)}_{\left(3;T,2\right)}&=\left[\frac{C_{F}C_{A}}{2}\right]\frac{1}{q^{-}}\frac{4y^{2}(1-2y+2y^{2})}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}}\frac{\left(1-\chi^{\prime}\right)}{\left(1+\chi^{\prime}\right)^{3}},\end{split}

(109)

where

\chi^{\prime}=\frac{M^{2}_{f^{\prime}}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}.

(110)

At sufficiently high
\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}
, Eq. (
109
) implies that producing heavy flavor
Q^{\prime}
is equally likely as light flavor
q^{\prime}
, that is

\lim_{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}\to\infty}\left[\frac{\mathcal{R}^{\left(q;Q^{\prime},\bar{Q}^{\prime}\right)}_{\left(3;T,2\right)}}{\mathcal{R}^{\left(q;q^{\prime},\bar{q}^{\prime}\right)}_{\left(3;T,2\right)}}\right]=1.

(111)

The longitudinal drag gives

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}^{\left(q;q^{\prime},\bar{q}^{\prime}\right)}_{\left(3;L,1\right)}&=-\left[\frac{C_{F}C_{A}}{2}\right]\frac{1}{\left(q^{-}\right)^{2}}\frac{2\left(1-3y+4y^{2}\right)}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}\left(1+\chi^{\prime}\right)}.\end{split}

(112)

Finally, in the situation where all diagrams in Fig.
7
participate, i.e. a light quark undergoes
q\to q+\bar{q}
, one obtains the following:

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}^{\left(q;q,\bar{q}\right)}_{\left(3;T,2\right)}&=\left[\frac{C_{F}C_{A}}{2}\right]\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\frac{4}{yq^{-}}\left[\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}}\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]-\frac{2\beta}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}+2\beta^{2}\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]\\
&+\left[\frac{C_{F}C_{A}}{2}\right]\frac{4}{q^{-}}\frac{y^{2}\left(1-2y+2y^{2}\right)}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}}\\
&+\left[C_{F}C_{A}\left(C_{F}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\right)\right]\frac{4\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{yq^{-}}\left[\frac{y}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}}\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]-\frac{2\beta y}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}+2\beta^{2}\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right],\end{split}

(113)

and

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}^{\left(q;q,\bar{q}\right)}_{\left(3;L,1\right)}&=\left[\frac{C_{F}C_{A}}{2}\right]\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\frac{1}{\left(yq^{-}\right)^{2}}\left[2\beta\left(\frac{1-2y}{1-y}\right)\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]\\
&-\left[\frac{C_{F}C_{A}}{2}\right]\frac{2}{\left(q^{-}\right)^{2}}\left[\frac{1-3y+4y^{2}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}\right]\\
&+\left[C_{F}C_{A}\left(C_{F}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\right)\right]\frac{\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{\left(yq^{-}\right)^{2}}\left[\frac{1-2y+2y^{2}}{1-y}\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}+2\beta\left(\frac{1-2y}{1-y}\right)\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right].\end{split}

(114)

V.4

participating in the process

depicted in Fig.

&\times\left[\left[1-2\left(\frac{3+\kappa y^{2}\chi}{1+\chi}\right)+6\frac{\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)}{\left(1+\chi\right)^{2}}\right]\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}}\right.\\
&\left.\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,\,+2\beta\left[3+\kappa y^{2}\chi-4\left(\frac{1+\kappa y^{2}\chi}{1+\chi}\right)\right]\frac{\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}\right.\\

and

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}^{\left(Q;Q,q^{\prime}\right)}_{\left(4;L,1\right)}&=\left[\frac{C_{F}C_{A}}{2}\right]\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\frac{1}{\left(yq^{-}\right)^{2}}\frac{2\left(1+\kappa y^{2}\chi\right)}{\left(1+\chi\right)^{2}}\times\\
&\times\left[\frac{2\chi}{\left(1+\chi\right)}\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}+\beta\left[\left(1+\chi\right)\left(\frac{1-2y}{1-y}\right)-2\chi\right]\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right].\end{split}

(116)

For the case of a light quark
q

in the two equations above.

Finally, in the case of a light quark

, then both channels in Fig.

contribute giving:

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}^{\left(q;q,q\right)}_{\left(4;T,2\right)}&=\left[\frac{C_{F}C_{A}}{2}\right]\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\frac{4}{yq^{-}}\left[\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}}-2\beta\frac{\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}+2\beta^{2}\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]\\
&+\frac{C_{F}C_{A}\left(C_{F}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\right)}{(1-y)yq^{-}}\,4\beta^{2}\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right],\end{split}

(117)

with

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}^{\left(q;q,q\right)}_{\left(4;L,1\right)}&=\left[\frac{C_{F}C_{A}}{2}\right]\left[\frac{1+\left(1-y\right)^{2}}{y}\right]\frac{1}{\left(yq^{-}\right)^{2}}\left[2\beta\left(\frac{1-2y}{1-y}\right)\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]\\
&-\left[\frac{C_{F}C_{A}}{2}\right]\left[\frac{1+y^{2}}{1-y}\right]\frac{1}{\left(q^{-}\right)^{2}}\frac{1}{\left(1-y\right)^{2}}\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}\\
&-\frac{C_{F}C_{A}\left(C_{F}-\frac{C_{A}}{2}\right)}{(1-y)}\,\frac{2}{\left(yq^{-}\right)^{2}}\left[\frac{\left[2-2\cos\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right]}{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}}+\beta\left(\frac{1-2y}{1-y}\right)\sin\left\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{0}\zeta^{-}\right\}\right],\end{split}

(118)

where
\beta
is defined in Eq. (
80
).

V.5
Length dependence of energy loss and its implications for jet phenomenology

We perform a numerical evaluation of the perturbative coefficients
\mathcal{R}^{(a;b,c)}_{(i;T,2)}
arising from the Taylor expansion of the full scattering kernel for kernel-1 and kernel-3. The collinear expansion [Eq. (
61
)] outlined in the preceding subsections enables us to decouple
k_{\perp}
and
k^{-}
dependencies, incorporating them into the definition of the jet transport coefficients. The length (
\zeta^{-}
) dependence only appears in the phase factors, such as
[2-2\cos\{\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}\}]
,
\beta
, and the two-point correlators
\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{(T,2)}
or
\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{(T,2)}
. Below, we discuss the length dependence based on the assumption that the two-point correlators
\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{(T,2)}
or
\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{(T,2)}
are invariant under translation around
\zeta^{-}
, thus focusing solely the
\zeta^{-}
dependence in
\mathcal{R}
.

First, a comparison between the results in Fig. 14 of Ref.
Sirimanna et al. 2022
, and our results for kernel-1 is presented in Fig.
9
.

(a)

\bar{\mathcal{K}}^{(q;q,g)}_{(1;T,2)}
at
y=0.25

(b)

\bar{\mathcal{K}}^{(q;q,g)}_{(1;T,2)}
at
y=0.5

(c)

\bar{\mathcal{K}}^{(q;q,g)}_{(1;T,2)}
at
y=0.75

Figure 9
:
Comparison of
\bar{\mathcal{K}}^{(q;q,g)}_{(1;T,2)}
among various approaches for massless jet quark.

To match our kernel with
\bar{K}
given in Eq. (76) of Ref.
Sirimanna et al. 2022
, we define
x=\boldsymbol{\ell}^{2}_{2\perp}(1+\chi)\beta=\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}\zeta^{-}=\frac{\zeta^{-}}{\tau_{f}}
, factorize the overall coefficient
C_{A}\left[\frac{1+(1-y)^{2}}{y}\right]\frac{4}{(1+\chi)^{3}\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}}
from Eq. (
81
), and take the massless
\chi\to 0
limit to give:

\bar{\mathcal{K}}^{(q;q,g)}_{(1;T,2)}=\lim_{\chi\to 0^{+}}\left[\frac{(1+\chi)^{3}\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}\mathcal{R}^{(Q;Q,g)}_{(1;T,2)}}{4C_{A}\left[\frac{1+(1-y)^{2}}{y}\right]}\right]=\frac{2-y}{2}\left[2-2\cos\left\{x\right\}-2x\sin\{x\}\right]+(1-n)x^{2}+\frac{C_{F}}{C_{A}}y^{2}.

(119)

Note that
\bar{\mathcal{K}}^{(q;q,g)}_{(1;T,2)}
almost reduces to
\bar{K}
in Ref.
Sirimanna et al. 2022
, except for the additional
\frac{C_{F}}{C_{A}}y^{2}
term stemming from the non-vanishing process depicted in Fig.
4
(c). For reference, the Aurenche, Zakharov and Zaraket (AZZ)
Aurenche et al. 2008a
;
Aurenche et al. 2008b
, as well as the Guo and Wang (GW)
Guo and Wang 2000
;
Wang and Guo 2001
curves are shown, with
\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{GW}=2-2\cos{x}
and
\bar{\mathcal{K}}_{AZZ}=2-2\cos{x}-2x\sin{x}+2x^{2}\cos{x}
. Our results are consistent with Sirimanna-Cao-Majumder (SCM) results for
y=0
. However, as
y
increases, such as
y=0.75
[Fig.
9
(c)], slight variations can be observed. Note that our calculation (KV,
n=1
) disfavors
n>1/2
, as the collision kernel
\bar{\mathcal{K}}^{(q;q,g)}_{(1;T,2)}
becomes negative in region
0.5<\zeta^{-}/\tau_{f}<2.4
. This is consistent with the phase-space constraint pointed out in Eq. (
41
), which puts the bound as
0\leq n<1/2
.

at

at

(c)

:

\bar{\mathcal{R}}^{(Q;Q,g)}_{(1;T,2)}
, a prefactor in front of
\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{(T,2)}
, defined in Eq. (

) assuming
\ell_{2\perp}=10
GeV, and
\tau^{-1}_{f}=\mathcal{G}^{(\ell_{2})}_{M}
.

To explore heavy-flavor energy loss in kernel-1, an interesting dimensionless quantity to look at is

\bar{\mathcal{R}}^{(Q;Q,g)}_{(1;T,2)}=\frac{\boldsymbol{\ell}^{4}_{2\perp}\mathcal{R}^{(Q;Q,g)}_{(1;T,2)}}{4C_{A}\left[\frac{1+(1-y)^{2}}{y}\right]}.

(120)

with
\mathcal{R}^{(Q;Q,g)}_{(1;T,2)}
given in Eq. (
81
). Figure
10
displays the mass dependence of
\bar{\mathcal{R}}^{(Q;Q,g)}_{(1;T,2)}
, a perturbative coefficient of the second-order gradient term
\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{(T,2)}
, characterizing Glauber-gluon
Idilbi and Majumder 2009
induced transverse momentum diffusion of a (heavy) quark as it traverses the nuclear medium.
Within the
{\rm\overline{MS}}

PDG
renormalization scheme, the heavy-quark masses are taken as
M=1.27
GeV for the charm quark and
M=4.18
GeV for the bottom quark. Our analysis shows that the charm quark exhibits no significant deviations in behavior when compared to lighter quarks, at a fixed
\ell_{2\perp}=10
GeV. Of course, given Eq. (
81
), this is not expected to hold as
\ell_{2\perp}\to 1.27
GeV. The bottom quark displays a pronounced impact, at
\ell_{2\perp}=10
GeV and for momentum fraction values exceeding
y>0.25
, highlighting the relevance of heavy-quark mass corrections in this kinematic regime. Similar behavior was observed in our earlier work for the photon Bremsstrahlung-based quark energy loss
Kumar and Vujanovic 2025
.

(a)

\bar{\mathcal{R}}^{(q;Q,\bar{Q})}_{(3;T,2)}
at
y=0.25

(b)

\bar{\mathcal{R}}^{(q;Q,\bar{Q})}_{(3;T,2)}
at

(c)

\bar{\mathcal{R}}^{(q;Q,\bar{Q})}_{(3;T,2)}
at
